# DEAS verification — native baseline, optional capabilities and languages

## Release 0.2.1 (published)

- GitHub release `v0.2.1` (https://github.com/darksidewalker/DEAS/releases/tag/v0.2.1), tag on source commit `f677e70e1369633fd3532cb0d66994faeb556a24` (tree `646a37b68c9f94f6a904432433bb8cda01e65e12`), pushed to both remotes. Built locally and unsigned with `build-linux.sh --release` (rustc 1.97.0, Node v26.12.1); hosted CI runners are unavailable, so no CI evidence.
- Gates on that tree: typecheck; frontend suite 1248 tests (one run hit a timing failure in the untouched `Forge.test.tsx` H3 group-card test under load; the file re-ran 79/79 green in isolation and the suite was green earlier on the same code); `cargo test --locked -- --test-threads=1` (650 lib tests); `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy --locked --release --lib --bins -D warnings`; `cargo xwin check --all-targets` and `cargo xwin clippy --all-targets -D warnings` for `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc`.
- Assets (downloaded back from the release and checked with `sha256sum -c`):
```
24f20096a9b8f5bdabbd823793d9cfa957df0fa99478ac505f1c2bd54103a99e  BUILD-MANIFEST.json
4bac6b0f69f97f8e332228506cb666d832d3aec3167e067825f864ae3ecd539a  deas.exe
0702c56374d5532a0e66341dc414bb8a1a83e760fded7eaae3cc49fe9e20fadc  deas-linux-amd64
```
- Linux binary: glibc floor 2.39; `ldd` reports no missing libraries. Both binaries embed the frontend chunk `index-CuOUItJn.js`; the Linux binary embeds `splash.js`. `deas.exe` is a cargo-xwin MSVC cross-build (benign LNK4099 warnings); no NSIS installer.
- Startup splash: probed natively on Linux in a headless `kwin_wayland --virtual` XWayland session with fresh XDG directories — dark splash with the localized interface phase, then the 1440×960 main window rendered after ~0.3 s. Not verified: warm start with a remembered environment, slow/failed restore, disabled autostart, Wayland-native, Windows; native audio-only libmpv playback of the YuE2 FLAC in the app (standalone `mpv` decodes it); per-job Stop against a real running job.

## 0.2.0 BETA — version and publication hygiene

- The current source moves from ALPHA to **BETA**; all six application version fields in the npm/Tauri/Cargo manifests and lockfiles agree on `0.2.0`. Existing platform/model acceptance limits remain unchanged. `package.json` keeps `private: true` to prevent accidental npm publishing; this is independent of GitHub repository visibility.
- Fresh checks: TypeScript typecheck, 125 core tests and locked offline Cargo metadata passed. Gitleaks 8.30.1 reported no credential findings in the local all-ref Git-history scan, the tracked/non-ignored working-tree export, or the decoded Forge SQLite/JSON bundles. Scanner success is not a guarantee that no sensitive data exists.
- Replaced personal Linux/Windows paths and private-remote details in documentation with neutral labels. The local-path fields in `assets/deas-native-automatic-review.png` and `assets/deas-first-start-0.1.38.png` are explicitly redacted; the remaining screenshot content is unchanged. Public attribution and synthetic security fixtures are retained.
- Publication is still a separate decision: previous paths remain in Git history, and one contributor uses a non-anonymized author email. No history rewrite, commit, push, visibility change, release build or native/model acceptance run was performed. The checkout has concurrent unrelated work; repeat the privacy scan against the frozen publication commit and review hosted release assets before making it public.

## Continuity enable/disable switch (0.1.67, shipped in 0.2.0)

Scope: `src/lib/continuity.ts`, `src/features/studio/{ContinuityPlanner,Workbench}.tsx`, studio locales (six languages), `ContinuityPlanner.test.tsx`, `Workbench.test.tsx`.

- Source check: the Director (`js/minimax_h3_director.js`) has no separate switch; `isContinuing()` is true while a source is selected and *Clear source* deactivates by clearing it. DEAS adds `plan.enabled` (off unless `true`, also for older saved plans) so switching off keeps source, session, steps and clips. Running fixtures enable it explicitly; the default-off Workbench test starts from a plan without the field.
- Tests: planner toggle keeps the draft, disables Run next/Run all, is locked while running; Workbench with a stored disabled plan shows **Continuity · off**, re-enabling persists `enabled:true` without a job, and an ordinary Run sends an H3 intent without `continuity` and leaves the stored plan untouched. Focused Vitest: Continuity planner/overlay files (30) and `Workbench.test.tsx -t Continuity` (7) passed; `npm run typecheck` passed. `npm run test:core` passed. The first full Vitest run had 1 failure out of 1341 tests (failing test not recorded). A repeat full run passed all 1341.
- No native/compiler change: execution intents are unchanged. Not verified: native Linux/Windows click-through.

## YuE2 music generation and covers (0.1.66)

Implemented the YuE2 text-to-music and cover pipelines end to end in the existing Studio boundaries: native recipe compilation with strict intent validation, music-scoped optional prerequisites (no image fallback), source preflight for covers, independent Full/Melody/Off prompt state, template-exact and seed/date naming snapshots resolved under the raw output prefix, immutable run snapshots across awaited uploads and UI edits, native FLAC result retention with history correlation, metadata and recent-history restore, and image-project guards. Six locales carry strict key/placeholder parity, and the Studio music tab is covered by the shared layout gate.

- `npm run typecheck` clean (app and test tsconfig).
- `npm run test:core`: 121/121 pass (node:test, includes the release-staging and music gate probes).
- `npm run test:ui`: 1320/1320 pass across 66 files (vitest) at the 0.1.66 commit; that run contained no music UI suites. The music UI regressions were added afterwards: `MusicPrompts.test.tsx` (independent editors, overlay, exact `YuE2 Song Style`/`YuE2 Lyrics` writer dialects, stale write after unmount), `MusicSettings.test.tsx` (planning modes, bounds, cover encoder), eight Workbench music flows (t2m intent without image fields, ABC Off/AR guidance, cover source selection + single upload + frozen intent, missing cover nodes/encoder, draft separation + Forge handoff refusal, music Run Plan, retained FLAC job player/metadata, history restore with explicit source re-selection) and music cases in `history.test.tsx`/`RunPlan.test.tsx`. See the follow-up gate run below.
- `npm run test:layout`: Chromium geometry PASS, 381 checks, 9 tabs, strict simulated IPC, negative controls included.
- Rust: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --locked --release --lib --bins -- -D warnings`, `cargo test --locked -- --test-threads=1` (679 library and 3 binary tests pass, 43 deliberately ignored) and `cargo xwin check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --all-targets` green at the release tree.

Open acceptance (tracked in [the plan](PLAN.md#43-music-yue2)): SheetSage2 list/multiplicity against a live backend, decoded-PCM memory-bounds probe, external-backend export fallback, real-model generation quality and scoped cancellation, and Linux/Windows native-playback evidence — browser fixtures are not native playback.

Follow-up gate run (music UI tests + `docs/MUSIC.md`): `npm run typecheck` 0 errors; `npm run test:core` 121/121; `npm run test:ui` 1337/1337 across 68 files (17 new music tests, all green on first full run after fixture fixes); `cargo fmt --check` clean; `cargo clippy --locked --lib --bins -- -D warnings` clean; `cargo test --locked --lib` 679 passed, 0 failed, 43 ignored (the earlier `downloads::strict_catalog_and_extension` 46-vs-43 mismatch was realigned in the 0.1.66 commit); `npm run build` OK; `npm run test:layout` PASS (381 checks, 9 tabs; the gate opens Generate in its default image pipeline — it does not switch to the music pipelines, so music control geometry at narrow widths is not covered by it); `git diff --check` clean. A6 evidence for the node schemas came from a throwaway CPU-only ComfyUI core `b1a247c9` (`--cpu`, port 8199, stopped afterwards, no live service touched): all four compiled graphs pass `validate_prompt`, `LoadAudio` decodes FLAC and MP4-with-audio and rejects a silent MP4 with `No audio stream found in the file.`, `SaveAudioAdvanced` reports FLAC under the `audio` history key. No weights were loaded and no song was generated; the real-model rows of D4 remain open.

## GGUF integration completion (0.1.65, shipped in 0.1.66)

Implemented bounded GGUF v2/v3 header parsing, native/diagnostic single and dual encoder routing, separate loader catalogs, complete mixed-format Run dependency checks, Wan LOW metadata/LoRA validation, and source-bound manual family declarations through the existing Studio/Library sidecar editor. GGUF is never a LoRA-file format. The nonexistent full-checkpoint loader was removed; GGUF diffusion weights need separate companions. GGUF VAE/audio-VAE/projection files are rejected, and standalone audio generation is not introduced. The installed quantized loader can still reject unsupported architectures after a manual family declaration; do not describe H3/MOSS/new architecture GGUF inference as verified.

New plans pin ComfyUI-GGUF `6ea2651e7df66d7585f6ffee804b20e92fb38b8a`. Explicit confirmed Upgrade/Reinstall extends legacy plans, preserves the original request/runtime profile, and publishes the new plan atomically only after successful dependency/import checks. Opening/starting never installs anything. Pin-derived SHA-256 source hashes cover every pack Python file and dependency declaration before package execution and again during approval/start validation. A real scratch Git regression rejects `core.worktree` redirection before any checkout writes. Independent reviews identified the source-integrity/worktree and mixed-companion/LOW-readiness gaps; each correction has an observed RED followed by passing regression checks.

Observed gates: 21 focused native GGUF tests passed (two live tests normally ignored), plus the explicitly executed pinned-source-content fixture passed against a real scratch clone and rejected subsequent module modification. The independent v2/v3 header fixture is included in seven bounded-parser regressions. Typecheck and 119 core tests passed. Targeted GGUF/LOW mounted UI tests passed; the final full UI run recorded 1,314 passes and one failure in the concurrently edited CLIP-vision Library download test (also reproduced in isolation). The initial full UI run before those concurrent edits passed all 1,310 tests. The Chromium shared-layout gate passed 381 checks. Strict all-target Clippy and Rust formatting passed. Windows all-target MSVC `cargo xwin check --locked --offline` passed; the first raw Cargo crosscheck lacked the configured MSVC C toolchain and failed in ring, not DEAS. This is cross-compilation evidence, not Windows execution.

The full unfiltered Rust gate retains the unrelated `downloads::tests::strict_catalog_and_extension` mismatch: the concurrently extended catalog contains 46 entries while its test expects 43. The remaining native library/binary/doc suites pass with only that test filtered. A timing-sensitive parallel LLM listing test failed once under concurrent load, then passed in isolation and on the next combined retry; no unrelated test or implementation was weakened. Keep these observations separate from GGUF acceptance.

A CPU-only probe initialized real ComfyUI and ComfyUI-GGUF without binding a server, verified all three loader registrations/return types and accepted the emitted image graph through `execution.validate_prompt` (`[true, null, ["7"], {}]`). Sentinel files were isolated in scratch and no tensors were loaded. The worker also exercised a real scratch pinned checkout/dependency install and GPU-uninitialized registration; this was not a full new managed Torch installation. No model weights were downloaded, user sidecars modified or live services restarted.

Open acceptance: full managed installer/migration execution, actual small image/video/audio-in-video model generation, performance/quality, Linux native WebView interaction and Windows runtime. The inspected ComfyUI model roots contained only 160-byte GGUF placeholders, not suitable diffusion weights. The execution environment has no DISPLAY/WAYLAND_DISPLAY, so native-window interaction was not exercised. Final Linux artifact staging and final review are recorded below when complete.

## Model-family metadata M-01 (0.1.65, shipped in 0.1.66)

Continued the existing uncommitted implementation without replacing unrelated work. Native selected-file checks run during owned-backend preparation, before consuming the plan, and again after the observer handshake immediately before prompt submission. External endpoints do not gain local machine-wide path authority. Reset accepts a structurally valid unknown family ID while preserving current fetched metadata; malformed JSON/fields remain non-destructive errors.

Regression evidence: `jobs_model_tests.rs` exercises checkpoint and LoRA replacement/corruption after a successful scan and after native preparation, with zero prompt POSTs; valid/undeclared files still submit, and external-backend preparation remains separate. A real loopback WebSocket handshake invalidates the metadata between validation and POST: the job becomes failed, its plan stays consumed, its observer completes cleanup and retry is rejected. `downloads_models.rs::tests::reset_unknown_family_retains_current_provider_metadata_without_hashing` uses a nonempty CivitAI response and verifies complete persisted JSON equality with the pre-declaration sidecar, exact fetched metadata retention, rescan equality and no additional hash read. Independent read-only review found no further defects in these two correction paths.

Integrated frontend evidence: TypeScript check, 119 core tests and 1,305 UI tests across 65 files passed. The shared Chromium viewport gate passed 381 checks. `tools/verify_model_metadata.py` passed 20 integrated checks: keyboard editing of a blocked unknown model, Library reset updating the still-mounted Studio, and real modal/Escape/geometry behavior in all six languages at 1440/768/320px. It uses strict simulated native IPC and rejects unexpected commands/network access, automatic generation/download/backend startup and provider/hash requests. Desktop and 320px German/English screenshots were inspected; fields wrap and all actions remain visible without overlaps. Results/screenshots: Hermes scratch `deas-postprocessing-2ovp3tsp/`. The first harness iterations incorrectly treated an unknown self-contained checkpoint as blocked and omitted the Library's Installed tab; correcting the fixtures/navigation made the gate pass without changing production UI behavior.

Native final gates: `cargo fmt -- --check`, strict all-target Clippy and `cargo test --locked` passed: 652 library tests and 3 binary tests, 42 library tests deliberately ignored; the nested one-test subprocess result is not counted twice. `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=.target ./build-linux.sh` completed and atomically staged `artifacts/deas-linux-amd64` (0.1.65), SHA-256 `e5b9ce30bf12e082af8a2d45e3a65590acdd2db1d41f27b6d1a01944aa231e1d`, equal to `.target/release/deas`; its frontend is `index-kEIxAaV8.js`. Relaunch the staged artifact to use it. No native window was launched in this run; the tool environment exposes no DISPLAY/WAYLAND_DISPLAY or Xvfb executable. Vite's existing chunk-size/mixed-import notices remain nonblocking. `git diff --check` passed.

Limits: browser IPC fixtures are not native WebView execution or model inference. Windows runtime, GTK/WebKit metadata interaction and real generation remain unverified. This slice does not resolve the separately documented GGUF parser/loader acceptance blockers. No model weights, user sidecars or live services were modified; no commit or push.

## Session-only pipeline and model drafts (0.1.64, shipped in 0.1.66)

The running Workbench remembers last-edited controls, prompts, input/reference media and source-bound editor masks/paint/layers by pipeline, exact model category/path and environment/project scope. It uses the existing validated live settings transfer and RAM-only media/canvas snapshots; no per-model browser-storage records or media copies are written. Ordinary global preferences still seed startup. Explicit project/history/metadata loading takes precedence, and submitted jobs remain unchanged.

After independent review, regressions also cover saving the outgoing active draft before environment changes (identical and different model paths), rejecting awaited magic-wand completion/error after draft/source/unmount changes, isolated ownership of restored canvases, and selecting an older image-to-image job while preserving remembered pipeline controls and the explicitly adopted source. Targeted Workbench/editor suites passed 296 tests. Full `npm test`, TypeScript checking and the shared Chromium layout gate passed again after these corrections. A separate isolated Chromium fixture exercised model/pipeline prompt and steps round trips, with no automatic native submission and no page errors. Its first harness attempt targeted the Load control before opening Models; the corrected harness passed without production changes.

Limitations: actual GTK/WebKit and Windows runtime interaction, real generation and memory/performance under many high-resolution painted drafts are not established. Cached media depends on original paths remaining available, and retained canvas snapshots consume session RAM. Full restart clears the cache; it is not persistent recovery. No services were restarted.

`./build-linux.sh` completed after the review fixes and atomically staged `artifacts/deas-linux-amd64` (SHA-256 `09140cdf8992a94daf0da25b56699e07fbd15a79dd6a51b580c5f6601e7e8807`). The staged binary contains the final frontend asset `index-oti86tvd.js`; relaunch is required. No commit or publication accompanies this verification.

## Learned video latent upscale (0.1.64, shipped in 0.1.66)

### Segment capture with latent upscale (0.1.64, shipped in 0.1.66)

The nodepack validates exactly one `DaSiWaH3ContinuitySegmentDecode` per graph, which the first W9 integration contradicted. The pairing now compiles inside DEAS without touching the installed pack: the single Segment Decode consumes the upscaled latent (`Segment Prepare → Tiled Upscale → Segment Decode`) and feeds Processed; the Original baseline is assembled from core nodes — `VAEDecode`/`VAEDecodeTiled` on the prepared window samples, `ImageFromBatch` cropping the window to the new segment, audio reused from the Segment Decode audio output, and `DaSiWaVideoCombine` on `raw/video/{prefix}_original`. The crop mirrors the pack's `render_layout` continuation timing (`source − 51·floor((source−102)/51)`, extension `floor(seconds·24/17 + 0.5)·17` with the Director's authored `durationSeconds`/`frames ÷ 24`); a comment records that a runtime crop divergence from a future pack fails closed at generation. Pinned checkpoint `sourceFrames` (grid `17k+5`, ≥5) is validated in the plan schema and resolved at prepare time from the session clip list (`/df_h3_continuity/sessions`), with plans that have no resolvable pin, first takes and imported-video pins rejected at compile. The Continuity UI no longer blocks the combination; the old six-locale blocker string was removed.

Gate rerun after these changes: `cargo test --lib` **631 passed / 42 ignored** (new graph test `h3_latent_upscale_segment_capture_decodes_the_baseline_on_core_nodes` covers the one-decoder chain, window crop math, authored-duration snapping, audio reuse, pair prefixes and the fail-closed states); `npm test` **119 core + 1,268 UI tests** across 63 files; six locale JSONs parse with no orphan keys and no unused-key audit hits. The new graph shape is compile-time verified only: real GPU execution, runtime crop equality against the pack, baseline-first ordering and the 16:9 target layout remain unverified (W5 scope).

Implemented within source/compiler, deterministic native tests and Chromium-fixture scope; real model/platform acceptance remains open. Source checked against ComfyUI `b1a247c9cf9dbfda4b2c0a0c1d50950667eeaa0c` and DaSiWa nodepack `ab364c582a3367eb3bab523b7b3b9d8c1fb17e63`. The owner's `DaSiWa MiniMaxH3 MythicAlchemy C-MMH3-33.json` SHA-256 is `3021589a75c35b2d535a146c5525307f6c0054fc09f9d746e24ef3a344931b0e`; node 2810 is `DaSiWaH3TiledUpscale`, between Segment Prepare 2796 and Segment Decode 2671, using Guide 2701 conditioning, prepared context, sampler/noise and the learned BF16 H3 upscaler at scale 2, one step and denoise 0.2. Core `nodes_lt_upsampler.py` supplies LTX's fixed-2× latent operation after the final sampling pass.

Integrated checks so far: the actual local ComfyUI `execution.validate_prompt` accepted all five emitted post-upscale graphs (H3 without capture, H3 full-timeline capture, and LTX with one/two/three initial passes), with both video savers and H3 Publish where applicable. This loaded the real node definitions in CPU mode but did not load weights or execute capture/inference. Evidence: scratch `deas-latent-upscale-graphs.json` and `deas-latent-graph-validation.log`. The import reported an existing optional colour/Matplotlib warning about missing `pyparsing`; graph validation still exited 0.

Integrated gates:
- `npm test`: final complete rerun passed **119 core and 1,268 UI tests**, 63 UI files. The earlier concurrent full run had the existing Workbench defaults assertion (20 vs 28); that failure also reproduced from an untouched `git archive HEAD`, and the isolated current retry plus final complete run passed. No unrelated defaults code/assertion was changed.
- `cargo test --locked`: **629 library and 3 binary tests passed**, 42 library tests ignored; doc tests had zero cases. Strict all-target Clippy, Rust formatting and the offline all-target Windows MSVC crosscheck passed. Nested subprocess summaries are not counted twice.
- Typecheck and production frontend build passed. Shared Chromium viewport gate passed **381 checks**. `tools/verify_latent_postprocessing.py` passed **54 checks across four family/installed-state cases**, all six languages at 1440/320px, with the real Workbench and Library panel but strict simulated IPC. It rejects download writes and unexpected external requests; selected files reveal the explicit existing Download action, and opening/cancelling never starts a download or generation. Final evidence: scratch `deas-latent-postprocessing-bxzykx4r/result.json`; desktop/narrow screenshots were inspected.
- Regression-first review fixes exclude LTX temporal-upscaler files from the spatial picker and keep the Library status callback stable through repeated Workbench renders. The latter test exercises running→succeeded polling and the subsequent availability refresh, not an immediate-success start response. Exact slash/backslash catalog identity, post-upscale PNG side-output bounds, encoded-video pair completeness, partial terminal retention, history/Run Plan and unchanged unpaired indices are covered.

Independent review fixes also register `latent_upscale_models` for primary/additional configured roots, route an absent final destination category through the existing anchored new-folder plan/start path, recover native download progress/cancellation on targeted popup remount and display rootless first/last PNGs at their exact native indices. Tests include a real loopback-fixture download into an initially absent category, no writes on planning/unconfirmed start, parent/collision/symlink rejection and close/reopen/cancel/completion. The browser gate now uses the real catalog entry IDs/base labels and requires automatic exact-file listing without clicking a card. Its simulated IPC cannot establish real HF download throughput or model inference.

`bash build-linux.sh` passed after review corrections and atomically staged `<workspace>/DEAS/artifacts/deas-linux-amd64` (0.1.64), matching `src-tauri/target/release/deas`: SHA-256 `dc1e40b9a90e3896fbe264cd68eb6cb030cba265416adc8af375200e6ae35866`. Quit/relaunch this artifact to load the new UI and owned-backend model paths; no nodepack change or live-service restart was performed. No commit, push or release publication.

Known limits retained from source review: the segment-capture pairing keeps one Segment Decode and assembles the Original baseline on core nodes, so no nodepack patch is needed; the compile-time crop mirrors the installed pack's timing and any future pack divergence fails closed at generation instead of silently mis-cropping. Independent saver branches do not guarantee original-before-upscale execution. Full clip latents still grow with duration despite internal temporal windows. Real learned-model inference, output quality, timing/audio decode acceptance, measured RAM/VRAM/throughput, target WebViews and Windows runtime remain unverified. No weights, services or source media are changed by these checks.

## Reference editors, video controls and preview More menu (0.1.62)

Committed at `e8e5e728` and shipped in release 0.1.62 (see section above). H3 selected references use static thumbnails plus current stream/crop summaries. Existing per-reference fields and ReferenceMedia are hosted in a second native dialog; source identity keeps that editor mounted across video-to-audio lane changes. Close/Escape stops the player, retains edits and restores the card's Edit focus. LTX reference editing and backend intent/pack formats are unchanged. Native video thumbnails are bounded cached FFmpeg frames at trim start, with a named/glyph fallback when extraction is unavailable; no WebKit video decoder is used for native thumbnails. Cache has no eviction policy.

Verified on Linux:
- `npm test`: 118 core and 1,217 UI tests passed, including persistence/reopen, nested cancel isolation, lane identity/focus and stale thumbnail completion tests. Workbench submission tests enter the new editor explicitly and retain exact intent assertions.
- `npm run build` / TypeScript clean. Shared layout gate: 381 checks passed (polling enabled for the owned Vite because the first earlier run hit the host inotify watcher limit).
- `cargo test --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --lib --locked`: 606 passed, 40 ignored. Strict all-target Clippy and formatting passed. The real FFmpeg thumbnail regression verifies different selected frames, invalid paths/times, source preservation and cache invalidation.
- `tools/verify_references.py`: all 18 locale/width combinations passed against real range-served synthetic MP4/WAV, with mocked native IPC. Nested focus trapping, real Escape, reopen persistence, mode/lane change, zero overview players and bounded crop playback passed. Final screenshots at 320/700/1440px inspected. Browser chrome can retain body focus after Tab; the background-focus rejection check first focuses the editor, then proves the outer opener cannot steal it.
- `tools/verify_native_references.py`: real owned GTK/Tauri dev instance on Wayland with libmpv; nested editor/crop/audio-mode/drop preservation passed, three framebuffer samples with changed hashes and no GL errors. Synthetic discovery headers only; no model load/inference or foreign service changes. The first native attempts correctly failed framebuffer acceptance: WebKitGTK overlay scrollbar hit-testing covered the video's right corners. Reserving additional end padding in the editor scroll body fixed visibility; the final probe explicitly checks all four corners plus framebuffer changes. Final evidence: scratch `deas-reference-native-editor-final/results.json`; Chromium evidence: scratch `deas-reference-editor-final-browser/results.json`.
- `./build-linux.sh` rebuilt `artifacts/deas-linux-amd64` at the release commit (0.1.62), SHA-256 `3f043fd7aff0c44981d11a3a87de39bfcc1ca5602046eac0619625dd50944c92`; the published copy lives in `artifacts/releases/v0.1.62/`. Relaunch the release binary to use the changes.

Not verified: Windows WebView2 runtime, audible audio/A/V sync, real H3 inference, general hostile-file sandboxing or resource/performance benchmarks. Originals and unrelated working-tree probes were preserved. No user-service restart.

## Reference overlay and media crops (0.1.59)

Scope: References moved from the dock to a native modal with the shared Media picker; Director lanes/labels/people/Forge details, packs and RefMods are retained. New inline previews and source-aware numeric/range crops, playhead endpoints/reset and Play crop use the same stream/trim values as H3 submission. Linux video reuses libmpv; standalone audio and Windows/browser retain media elements. No new dependencies, model downloads, source rewrites, commits, publication or live-service restarts.

Evidence collected on Linux:

- `npm test`: 118 core and 1,213 UI tests passed (60 UI files) on the final reviewed tree. Scoped reference/native/media suites also passed. Native/component tests cover mute/audio-only behavior, crop IPC, source/mode cleanup, source-duration validation, aggregate stream totals and both-stream reference counting.
- `cargo test --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --locked --lib`: 603 passed, 40 ignored, no failures. Strict all-target Clippy and Rust formatting passed.
- `npm run test:layout`: 381 Chromium geometry checks passed, including the deliberately broken negative control, retained navigation, locales and narrow windows. This is not Windows/WebKit runtime acceptance.
- `tools/verify_references.py`: 18 Chromium cases across en/de/fr/tr/ja/zh and widths 1440/700/320. Real synthetic eight-second MP4/WAV files with HTTP range support: video crop 1–3 s and audio crop 2–4 s advance and stop at the selected end; picture-only is muted, both is unmuted, sound-only hides video and retains crop. Native modal/focus return, nested overflow and no upload/submission assertions passed. Backend IPC is mocked in this suite.
- `tools/verify_native_references.py`: actual DEAS debug frontend under Tauri/GTK on Wayland, actual shared media discovery and small safetensors-header architecture fixtures (never loaded for inference), actual libmpv rendering. A picker-selected video opens inside the real References modal, plays crop 1–5 s, switches to sound-only with the same crop and plays/stops again, then Close returns opener focus. Three framebuffer samples change with `gl_error=0`. Evidence: scratch `deas-references/native-overlay-reviewed/{native.log,results.json}`. Loopback port 1 is intentionally unavailable: no backend or generation is involved.
- Independent direct GTK/libmpv crop probe: video and audio-only both stop at 1.199979 s for requested end 1.2 s; ordinary seek/replay clears the file-local end, hidden audio advances, video restoration produces changing framebuffer samples with no GL error. Evidence: scratch `deas-gtk-crop-probe/native.log`.
- `./build-linux.sh` staged `artifacts/deas-linux-amd64` atomically at 0.1.59. The running user app retains its old inode until quit/relaunch; build staging is not a restart. Final staged SHA-256: `9c66ddde0165572acbbb3ad61323844992801f8e962c0a067eb538c9385b4129`.
- Independent review found a stale native-drop callback in the shared MediaLibrary. A current callback ref, completion-after-unmount guard and modal drop ownership now preserve later reference additions/trims and leave background browsers untouched. A regression failed before the fix and passed afterward; the native modal probe additionally adds B, drops C, and checks that both B and A’s 1–5 s crop remain.
- One full-suite run concurrent with the release build failed the pre-existing relocation-selection timing test; that entire media file passed twice in isolation, and the complete final `npm test` rerun passed. This is recorded as a transient verification failure, not a production relocation repair.

Probe setup failures were isolated: the initial native process had no display environment; the Wayland retry found the session socket. A plain `cargo build` embeds production assets, so the fixture page needs a Tauri dev build/launcher using the owned Vite URL. Native IPC properties are immutable, so the final native probe uses real discovery and header fixtures, not an attempted frontend IPC override. Browser synthetic HTTP needed range responses to support real seeking. These fixture/harness fixes are not production playback repairs.

Limits: waveforms, thumbnail strips, whole-crop dragging, independent AV crops and alternate embedded track selection remain open. Source duration is advisory UI metadata; missing streams and unknown durations still require backend decoding. Native audio-only/video timing and mute state were checked, not audible output or AV sync. No real H3/model computation, long-owner-media stress run, or Windows WebView2/runtime acceptance was performed. Originals and unrelated working-tree edits/services were preserved.


## H3 continuity pipeline parity (0.1.58)

- Compared nodepack `ab364c582a3367eb3bab523b7b3b9d8c1fb17e63`, Core `b1a247c9cf9dbfda4b2c0a0c1d50950667eeaa0c` and the owner's `DaSiWa MiniMaxH3 MythicAlchemy C-MMH3-33.json` (SHA-256 `3021589a75c35b2d535a146c5525307f6c0054fc09f9d746e24ef3a344931b0e`). Director 2730 is REF2VA; Guide 2701, sampler 2591, Prepare 2796, Decode 2671, exporter 2568 and Publish 2797 establish the shared continuity chain. No workflow/nodepack edits were made.
- Enabled H3 Continuity in all video pipelines and wired REF2VA capture/checkpoint/imported-video continuations through the existing native graph. Strict optional `useReferences` is default-off and shared with Forge, whose native boundary accepts true only for REF2VA. Tail-only continuation skips unused uploads/RefMods and permits empty media, without weakening malformed-input, model or normal-run guards. Source family/canvas/FPS compatibility is enforced by the Director; FL2VA and REF2VA are not interchangeable.
- Added source-backed optional REF2VA `durationSeconds` only for actual continuations. It forwards authored seconds to Director.duration; absent values preserve old graphs. The extracted real upstream timing function confirms the reason: authored 6 seconds yields 136 new frames, whereas a roundtrip through a 158-frame new-take intent yielded 153. The old non-REF2VA timing path is not changed by this extension.
- Integrated typecheck, **118 core / 1206 UI tests**, and **381 shared-layout checks** passed. The full UI gate includes concurrent References/NativeVideo work, not just continuity; jsdom emitted unimplemented media-pause warnings from that separate work despite all tests passing. Earlier partial runs collided with files being created or temporarily inconsistent types; no production changes were made to suppress those tests.
- Full serial native `--lib --bins` tests passed **606**, with **40 ignored**; strict lib/bin/example Clippy and rustfmt passed. Focused RED/GREEN coverage includes REF2VA source/reference options, empty media, exact duration, validation, captured intent/sequencing and Forge HTTP forwarding. Windows-MSVC offline all-target crosscheck passed on the then-current shared tree (another session had advanced its version to 0.1.59); this is not Windows runtime acceptance.
- Chromium Continuity acceptance passed **108 pipeline/locale/viewport cases** on a frozen scratch snapshot: all four H3 video pipelines, six languages, three widths, checkpoint/input-video/local-upload flows, default-off reference toggles, nested Forge, focus and retained drafts, with strict simulated IPC and no generation. Updated the old fixture for current native collection/LLM-read commands. Snapshotting was necessary because concurrent Vite HMR detached controls during the first live-tree run.
- `bash build-linux.sh` rebuilt and atomically staged the local 0.1.58 daily binary, SHA-256 `cdaa048c91e349d9ae29164add094b9798c28181497f458b60807bc30edbb961`. It includes the shared checkout's concurrent frontend work; it is not an isolated continuity-only artifact or published release. No running app/backend was restarted.
- Independent scoped review returned no security or logic blockers. Its duration suggestions were then covered: Workbench asserts authored REF2VA seconds (including six seconds), native tests reject duration fields on first takes/non-REF modes, and metadata/Run Plan show requested seconds instead of misrepresenting the new-take frame count. The 262 affected UI tests and native duration regression passed after these final changes; typecheck and the production build passed again. The final shared-tree 0.1.59 daily binary is `artifacts/deas-linux-amd64`, SHA-256 `c3b556cc23e5f3a43e34645c4a0520af8c243154e264de95984f01bfd8e788fc`. Scoped continuity whitespace checks pass; an unrelated trailing-blank-line finding in the concurrently edited ReferencesPanel test was left untouched.
- Remaining: real H3 GPU generation/continuation seam quality, source VAE identity, native GTK/WebKit and Windows runtime interaction. Upstream import caching does not fingerprint VAE weights; keep codec weights stable within a session. LTX/Wan are not supported by H3 latent continuity. The existing non-REF timing roundtrip is outside this bounded extension.

## Windows runtime: Chat and Prompt Forge (0.1.53 release build)

Contributor-run native Windows pass of the published `deas.exe` (SHA-256 `cbfa1c7c04aca488874064eb573069d130dd8c45267bdff4dfa8d91797bf54f9`, matching `SHA256SUMS`) on Windows 10 / WebView2 with an RTX 5080, existing environment, shared models folder. LLM: a remote OpenAI-compatible llama-swap server (Qwen3.8 27B Q4_K_P) reached through an SSH tunnel. Driven through the WebView2 DevTools port; screenshots inspected.

Accepted on Windows:
- Launch and owned-backend autostart (`Owned backend · Installed · Started`); ComfyUI 0.42.0 on cuda:0. Startup `object_info` BACKEND_UNAVAILABLE/BACKEND_TIMEOUT warnings while ComfyUI loads reach central Logs, rate-limited.
- Chat: server model list with a default selection; a send runs the `write_prompt` tool and renders the Illustrious card with Copy and Send to Generate; `search_tags` rounds reach Logs. Streaming shows partial text; **Stop** mid-reply returned Send, kept the partial reply, and the server GPU fell from 97 % to 0 % within 2 s (cancellation reaches the server).
- Prompt Forge tab, Illustrious: one Write in about 20 s with local canon (`frieren via unique name`), three tag-lookup rounds and negative clean-up; positive and negative filled; builder controls visible once models loaded. **Send prompt + negative to Generate** filled Studio's Prompt and Negative fields.
- Studio hammer: follows the selected checkpoint (`Write for: Anima`), pre-fills the current prompt, shows Writing…, then Current/New with Use/Keep; Use replaced the prompt with Anima-style output (`safe, 1girl, solo, frieren, …`).

Findings:
- Fixed (Unreleased): the server-required warning showed in Chat, the Forge tab and the hammer while a working server was connected, because `ForgeButton.tsx` added it whenever any local GGUF was discovered. It now appears only when local GGUFs are the only models; `ForgeModels.test.tsx` covers both cases.
- Open: an Illustrious result from the Forge tab can be sent to Generate while an Anima checkpoint is selected, with no warning. The stale/incompatible Send guard on the unmerged `feat/deas-recent-history` branch may cover it; not changed here to avoid overlapping that branch.
- Open: Chat answered "write an Illustrious prompt, then explain the tag order in one sentence" with the prompt card only; the explanation was dropped after the tool call.
- Not reproduced: the first Chat message after launch was sent with a stray leading `t`; later sends were clean.

Not covered: the builder with no reachable LLM, image generation, vision/i2i hammer, MOSS Layout write.
## Windows runtime: TVE source inspection and output draft (0.1.53 release build)

Contributor-run native Windows pass of the published `deas.exe` (SHA-256 `cbfa1c7c04aca488874064eb573069d130dd8c45267bdff4dfa8d91797bf54f9`) on Windows 10 / WebView2, ffprobe 8.1 (gyan.dev full build) on PATH through a WinGet link. The native chooser was answered through UI Automation/window messages; results were read through the WebView2 DevTools port. Sources were synthetic ffmpeg clips only.

Accepted on Windows:
- MP4/H.264/AAC at 30000/1001 (exact rational rate, one audio stream), WebM/VP9 without audio, 1920×1080 at 60 fps, plain MKV/H.264/AAC, and a file in a folder named with spaces and `ü` all inspected correctly.
- Blockers: `yuv444p` reports *Pixel format is unsupported*; a 90° display matrix reports *Source rotation is unsupported*; every clip reports unknown color metadata. A text file named `.mp4` is refused with `VIDEO_TYPE` (container signature mismatch). Chooser cancel and failed inspections keep the previous result; each inspection writes one `video-post` line to central Logs.
- TVE output draft at 1×/2×/4× gives the expected dimensions (512×288 → 512×288 / 1024×576 / 2048×1152; 1920×1080 at 4× → 7680×4320, within the 8192 limit), keeps the source rate exactly and maps AAC audio or *No audio*.

Fixed (Unreleased): about two in three inspections of valid files failed with `VIDEO_PROBE_FAILED` on Windows. `win::exited` reads the exit code, which Windows sets before the process object is signalled; `terminate()` then ran `TerminateJobObject(job, 1)` inside that window and the probe's 0 became 1. `run_probe` now waits for the already-exited probe before the job kill and uses that status. A repeated-inspection loop through the production helper failed 33/50, 32/50 and 36/50 on three clips before the change and 0/50 on each of five clips after it. The ignored `real_ffmpeg_clip_inspected_by_production_native_helper` smoke test now repeats the inspection 20 times; on Windows it failed at its first inspection before the change and passes after it.

Windows native suite (`cargo test --locked -- --test-threads=1`): 440 passed, 29 ignored, 4 failed, identical before and after the fix. The four are long-standing Windows-only failures, not caused by this change: `llm::tests::python_worker_mocked_import_fixtures`, `media::star::tests::recognized_media_moves_nested_arbitrary_bytes_without_parsing`, `projects::tests::create_cancel_save_close_reopen_and_single_writer`, `settings::tests::extra_model_roots_register_beside_the_primary_root`. `cargo fmt --check` and `cargo clippy --locked --lib -- -D warnings` pass.

Not covered: real user media, HDR/interlaced/alpha/non-square sources, ffprobe missing from PATH, a fix-build GUI pass of the inspection (the fix was verified through the production helper in a loop, not by clicking).

## TVE workspace and native spatial core (0.1.53)

- Main TVE navigation, retained source/draft and separate image Post-Processing integration are implemented. Independent read-only review reported no must-fix findings in this metadata-authoring slice; this is not approval of video execution.
- Parent execution: 107 core tests, 978 UI tests, frontend typecheck/production build; all passed. The core gate initially rejected hardcoded brand/profile JSX; labels now use all six dictionaries and the complete rerun passed.
- Native default-feature suite: 522 library tests and 3 binary tests passed (34 explicitly ignored library tests). The later integrated `video-upscale` feature suite passed 531 library tests and 3 binary tests (39 explicitly ignored). Parent explicitly ran the four native-FFmpeg streaming CPU-callback tests: all passed. Format, strict production/example Clippy and the offline Windows-MSVC feature/library/tests/examples crosscheck passed after local style/conditional-import fixes. The first chained Windows command did not execute after Clippy failed; the separately logged final Windows run is the evidence.
- `tools/verify_video_post.py`: 24 strict simulated-IPC Chromium checks passed across six languages and 1440/960/320-pixel widths, including explicit chooser/draft actions, exact rational metadata, edit invalidation, navigation retention and error/retry. Desktop German and narrow French screenshots were inspected. Fixture coverage is not native chooser/WebKitGTK/Windows/GPU acceptance.
- Host-native CPU codec smoke: three tiny synthetic 64×48 clips at 30000/1001 were encoded, probed and fully decoded with MP4/H.264/AAC, MKV/H.264/AAC and WebM/VP9/Opus using 8-bit yuv420p. Artifacts: `<verification-scratch>/deas-tve-codecs-af3ba0d1/`. These verify local codec combinations only, not the unconnected DEAS render path, A/V preservation for user media or AI quality/performance.
- Linux default application rebuilt successfully as `artifacts/deas-linux-amd64`, version 0.1.53; ELF x86-64, SHA-256 `7d6b93e529f16aeee0b04dc3f8404e39527be724fe8c5d63dc99821282dbd791`. No publication or native UI launch occurred. The default build excludes optional model/GPU initialization.
- Real native CUDA spatial proof: two consecutive 8×8 → 16×16 frames on one session, finite outputs, no CPU fallback; separate hardware test and actual PNG CLI succeeded. Exact initialization cause was the installed provider's unresolved `cudnnGetConvolutionBackwardDataAlgorithm_v7`, not demonstrated OOM: process-scoped `LD_PRELOAD=/usr/lib/libcudnn.so.9` resolved it without installs, loader changes or a product workaround. Parent read back matching actual source/output SHA-256: `f34d139cae120da5175b7642227154c7d9504b85c98a9df5bc8b2402ad3de8e2` and `12c1fdbd273affbf2b19e8fd396d1ad88e0491d7acfe8bc509a3a5eab3670215`; files are under `<verification-scratch>/ort-cuda-proof/`. This proves the PNG primitive, not end-to-end AI video, perceptual quality or measured peak resource use.
- The new Linux-only developer streaming core is bounded to ten seconds/600 frames/120 fps, 512-pixel inputs, 2048-pixel outputs and 256 MiB files. Complete frame timing and fixed properties, AAC mapping, encoded output decode, source preservation, no-clobber races, failure/cancellation and owned-child reaping are tested with actual native codecs and explicitly non-AI CPU callbacks. End-to-end real-AI video is still open; the GPU became occupied again, so no competing video inference was started. Independent read-only streaming review found no must-fix issues within the trusted Linux CLI scope and independently ran six focused CPU tests, including the real-codec cases. It also verified that installed FFmpeg implicit tagged BT.709 YUV→RGB conversion matches explicit BT.709 rather than BT.601. Known limits: initial source inspection can delay cancellation by its separate ten-second timeout; synchronous model calls cannot be preempted; the published directory entry is not fsynced, so atomic no-clobber publication is not a crash-durability guarantee.
- Draft `processingReady`, `timingVerified` and `encoderVerified` always remain false. No model selection, execution authority, video Run, preview/queue, output file or JSON import/export is provided. W8a2/W8b remain open; bounded Rust streaming/inference work is a separate in-progress slice.

## Rust video source inspection (0.1.52)

PP2a/W8a1 is implemented as a bounded source-inspection feature, not video inference/rendering or full W8a preparation. The owner’s standalone-video requirement is now explicit in the project rules and plans: Rust processing/inference integration, no Go or Python worker. Existing ComfyUI image recipes remain separate.

- New `video_post.rs::native_video_post_inspect(title)` accepts only a validated native chooser title. No frontend source/executable/argv is accepted. Registration, module export, dedicated permission and main-window capability are wired; `src-tauri/README.md` documents the seam. After selection it checks supported container magic and stable regular local-file identity, rejects symlinks/reparse/device/network paths as applicable, and runs native FFprobe with fixed demuxer/protocol limits, no MOV external data references, bounded probing/allocation/stdout/stderr and a 10-second deadline. A single native permit prevents overlapping inspection; its blocking owner cleans/reaps the owned process even if the IPC waiter disappears. Source metadata inspection is not a security sandbox against hostile same-user filesystem mutation.
- Exact rational-rate parsing avoids float-derived FPS; dimensions, stream counts, known duration and pixel/color/field metadata are validated. Conservative blockers cover cadence, field order, pixel aspect, dimensions, pixel format, alpha, rotation/display matrices and HDR/wide-gamut/unknown color. Matching header rates do not prove CFR; `timingVerified:false` and `processingReady:false` are unconditional. Shared Logs receive fixed severity/origin/operation UUID/outcome, not source paths/names or raw tool stderr.
- The Post-Processing component invokes only on an explicit chooser click, preserves the previous result on cancel, excludes duplicate clicks, ignores unmounted replies and shows errors without changing image settings or submitting work. Its snapshot is view-local. Independent source review found missing frontend SAR blocker labels; `nonSquarePixels` and `pixelAspectUnknown` now have six-language labels and are included in the focused UI matrix/browser cases. No remaining native source-review blocker was reported. The existing Windows spawn-before-Job-assignment limitation remains disclosed; Windows execution is not established by source review.

Fresh integrated results:

- **107 core tests and 944 UI tests (52 files)** passed; **19 focused source-inspection UI tests** passed separately, including all localized blocker codes, cancel/error/retry, duplicate clicks, invalid native payloads and unmounted replies. TypeScript check and production frontend build passed.
- **513 native library tests passed, 34 ignored; 3 binary tests passed**, serially. Nine ordinary source-inspection tests include parser/identity/log/admission and real owned-subprocess timeout/stdout/stderr/nonzero-exit cases. The real codec smoke is intentionally ignored in the ordinary suite because FFmpeg is an optional runtime dependency, not an implicit CI requirement.
- The ignored production-helper smoke was explicitly run and **passed** using actual installed FFmpeg/FFprobe: a generated three-frame `64×48` SDR H.264 clip named with Unicode/spaces returned `30000/1001`, duration `0.1001`, `yuv420p`, progressive, BT.709, empty blockers and both readiness flags false. Byte-for-byte source preservation was asserted. The fixture uses scratch only, without Python/Go workers, model loading or GPU work. Log: `deas-video-source-native-smoke.log` in Hermes scratch.
- Rust formatting, strict production-library/binary Clippy and offline Windows-MSVC library/test crosscheck passed. The previously documented unrelated all-test-target Clippy warning in `install.rs` is not claimed fixed or rerun clean. Linux `./build-linux.sh` rebuilt and atomically staged `artifacts/deas-linux-amd64` at 0.1.52; no running instance was restarted. Existing Vite chunk/import warnings remain.
- `tools/verify_video_post.py` reuses the existing strict collection fixture and owned Vite/browser runner. **21 Chromium checks** passed, including explicit-only inspection, cancel preserving result, error/retry/busy exclusion and six locales at 1440/960/320px with real pointer activation and five blocker labels including SAR. Native chooser/probe results are simulated IPC: this does not prove actual native chooser/export/filesystem behavior. Initial desktop German and narrow French screenshots were inspected; final artifacts are in `deas-postprocessing-i8n4b34t/`. No unexpected IPC/network or generation/stop/upload calls; owned Vite cleanup completed.

Not accepted: native Linux chooser/WebKitGTK interaction, actual Windows/WebView2/filesystem behavior, full packet/frame CFR verification, AI model/runtime support, streamed decode/inference/encode, audio/subtitle synchronization, HDR output, collision-safe processed-video publication, controlled RAM/VRAM/throughput or a full enhancement job. Source inspection never advertises a runnable pipeline. FFprobe must be installed on DEAS’s PATH; no automatic installation, dependency/model download, external service or source-repository change, commit/push/tag/release occurred. Prior PP1 work and owner splash-plan edits remain intact.

## Paired image post-processing (0.1.51, shipped in 0.1.52)

Bounded PP1 integration, not full post-processing acceptance. Native/TypeScript compilers save the composed pre-refine image at `B7` and final output at `7`, applying the effective saver/format/compression to both. Immutable graph-derived roles preserve final-first compatibility; failed/cancelled jobs retain reported validated outputs, and a success missing one paired role becomes failed. Native PNG transfer uses role-specific baseline/refine dimensions and existing index/cache/root/transport/decode/export guards. UI offers six-language role selection and comparison against the actual baseline; ordinary no-refine paths stay unchanged. The standalone non-refine Post-process action is not duplicated.

Fresh integrated verification:

- **107 core tests / 925 UI tests (51 files)** passed; TypeScript check and production frontend build passed. Focused preview tests reproduce the initial native-fetch selection race (two failures before correction) and now serialize latest-index auto-loading after an obsolete request succeeds or rejects, discarding stale bytes/errors. Initial independent review found that race; focused independent re-review found no remaining blockers and separately passed all 16 preview tests plus typecheck. Ordinary auto-error retry and stale-save resumption were source-reviewed, not separately regression-tested.
- **504 native library tests passed, 33 ignored; 3 binary tests passed**, run serially through `cargo test --locked -- --test-threads=1`. Rust formatting and strict production `--lib --bins` Clippy passed. `--all-targets` strict Clippy remains blocked by the already documented unrelated `install.rs:2196` needless borrow in a test; it was not changed to disguise this gate. Windows-MSVC library/test crosscheck passed offline; this is not Windows runtime acceptance.
- Real offline **CPU ComfyUI executor** probe was rerun by integration against source `b1a247c9cf9dbfda4b2c0a0c1d50950667eeaa0c`, with synthetic 64×64 pixels and no model/service. It validated and executed both savers, decoded their PNGs, retained `B7` on a deliberate late failure and interruption, and demonstrated **zero saved outputs on an independent early loader failure**. The probe exits zero only if all four observed outcomes match. Scratch evidence: `deas-pp1-cpu-executor.log`, `deas-paired-executor-probe.py`, `deas-paired-executor-wvxsw_wp/`. This exercises actual executor/saver behavior, not real refinement quality.
- `tools/verify_postprocessing.py` imports the strict existing collection fixture and owns an isolated Vite child. Its initial run exposed overlapping role selectors and floating edit tools; a dedicated auto grid row fixed the actual pointer interception. Fresh Chromium run passed **16 functional/layout checks**, six languages at 1440/320px, actual role/queue clicks, true baseline/result comparison, retained failed/cancelled outputs, exact rootless native job/index preview/export calls and unchanged ordinary jobs. Product findings are empty. Synthetic IPC/media/export responses do not establish actual native export or filesystem behavior. Desktop English and narrow French screenshots were inspected; scratch results/screenshots are in `deas-postprocessing-sz7xsu8v/`. Owned Vite cleanup completed.
- Linux `./build-linux.sh` rebuilt and atomically staged `artifacts/deas-linux-amd64` with 0.1.51 and the integrated frontend. No user instance was restarted. Existing Vite chunk-size/import warnings remain. No commit, push, tag or release.

Open acceptance: sibling saver scheduling is **not a completion dependency**, so this does not guarantee a baseline before every refine-loader failure. The complete paired-preservation contract stays open in PP1; do not infer it from the passing pair/late-failure cases. WebP native transfer remains unsupported (checked local paths retain their existing behavior). Real AI refinement/upscale quality, controlled before/after RAM/VRAM/throughput, native WebKitGTK/WebView2 interaction, Windows runtime/filesystem acceptance and video-worker execution remain unverified. No model download, dependency-profile change, external checkout edit or live-service change was performed. The owner’s pre-existing splash-plan documentation edit is preserved.

Video reconnaissance inspected TrueVideoEnhancer `67ddb2508fdb6da7f1897bbd964f316592f762d0` read-only and split W8 into imported-video plan, first owned spatial worker and later generated-video/effects slices. TVE bounded queues/two-endpoint processing are useful, but rawvideo timing/color, cancellation deadlines, final cancellation-before-publication, decoded readback and cross-process GPU scheduling must be verified independently. Core ComfyUI tiling/rebatching retains batch-sized allocations; a separate process is not itself a memory optimization. No performance superiority is claimed.

## H-02A: owned preview-observer lifetime (0.1.55)

2026-10-28, Windows local checkout. Actual branch `feat/deas-recent-history`, full HEAD/base `49505ba3cc1aba164d0f3fdec37f48f3c609be03`, the compatible owner-created U-01/U-02 checkpoint on top of Batch B. Initial status was only the owner's unrelated `.gitignore` edit; index empty. The checkpoint was retained. `.tools/H02A-initial-working-tree-2026-10-28.json` records branch/HEAD/status, initial diff and 574 tracked/planning/prior-review artifact hashes before H-02A edits.

Ownership design: a private controller lives in the job's shared `Live`. `Job` clones share that owner; the observer holds only a weak live reference, cancellation receiver and independent completion acknowledgement. It starts at most once. Native terminal submission/status/scoped-cancellation commits and confirmed aggregate Stop invalidation signal idempotent cleanup. Final live owner release also wakes connection/receive/control-flush waits. Ordinary unknown status, temporary clones, remounts and abandoned IPC waiters do not revoke still-owned active observation. The socket drops before completion acknowledgement; graceful Close remains bounded to one second, without socket I/O or joins under native mutexes. Publication rechecks cancellation under the same Live lock. Output/cache/history/intent, polling and execution/cancellation authority remain unchanged. Fixed redacted shared warnings identify operation and canonical client UUID where available, with per-operation rate limiting; closure alone never publishes an execution outcome.

Completed-package checkpoint (source-only evidence, **not native execution**):

| Executed check | Exact result |
| --- | --- |
| `Get-Command cargo,rustfmt,rustc,node,python -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue` and Python `shutil.which` | Node/Python available; Cargo, rustc and rustfmt unavailable. No installation or native check attempted |
| `git diff --check` | Passed, exit 0; log `.tools/H02A-2026-10-28-whitespace.log` |
| Python standard-library scoped source/preservation scan | Passed: tracked/untracked trailing whitespace and final newlines, 21 authored `watch_` function names, changed documentation link paths, allowed scope, initial protected hashes, unchanged branch/HEAD and empty index. Final snapshot has 564 of 574 baseline files byte-identical; only ten scoped existing files changed, with three new native files. `.gitignore`, plans, U-01/U-02 sources/locales/fixtures, history/persistence/outputs implementations, manifests/locks/workflows/version and original review artifacts are preserved |
| Complete scoped diff inspection and forward/reverse in-memory patch-content/blob checks | Passed against actual HEAD/current contents, including all three additions. No Git patch application or index mutation |

Authored **17 new native regressions**: 11 observer lifecycle/integration cases in `jobs_observer_tests.rs`, five Stop/cancel cases in `jobs_observer_stop_tests.rs`, and one shared redaction/rate-limit logging case in `jobs_observer.rs`. The four existing `watch_completion_sends_close_frame`, `watch_ignores_foreign_completion_and_progress_but_answers_pings`, `watch_close_wait_is_bounded_without_holding_live_mutex` and `watch_answers_peer_close` cases retain their names/behavior and now await actual cleanup completion. The `watch_` selector has **21 source cases; zero native tests executed**. Existing `preview_frames_need_known_format_and_magic` coverage is preserved.

Fixtures cover silent/pinging sockets while REST commits success/failure/interruption; final owner release and incomplete connection cancellation; temporary Job/Jobs clones; dropped Run/poll/Stop waiters; unknown reconciliation; independent A/B/new owners and diagnostic replacement; submission rejection; cancellation races without later accepted frames; partial/authority-refused Stop, retained products/history and exact mutation counts. The repeated original-controller mixed HTTP/WebSocket fixture performs 25 scratch admissions/terminal commits/evictions, requires 25 actual cleanup acknowledgements and retains the twenty-entry queue, ten-entry history and outputs. These are **unexecuted assertions**, not observed zero-survivor or memory results. Five hook-specific Stop/cancel cases reuse their original HTTP-only authority fixture: only after its optional 404 connection attempt has completed, a test-only controller replacement attaches a real socket. Main lifecycle/admission fixtures use the original observer without replacement; production never rearms or reconnects an observer.

Pending in `src-tauri/` with an equipped existing toolchain: `cargo fmt --all -- --check`; `cargo clippy --locked --all-targets -- -D warnings`; `cargo test --locked --lib watch_`; `cargo test --locked --lib preview_frames_need_known_format_and_magic`; affected `jobs::tests::cancel_tests`, `whole_stop_`, `workbench_poll_` and `jobs::history::tests` selectors; and `cargo build --locked`. ## Unified image reference wiring (0.1.47)

- Integrated local acceptance: UI attachments, immutable ordered uploads, strict TS/native schemas, prerequisite discovery and both graph compilers are wired. Qwen references use the core node's latent for t2i and retain the source latent/denoise for i2i; Krea/MOSS use the bundled text/vision adapter without unrelated latent or geometry changes. Missing dependencies block Run. No Swarm installation, model download, foreign-backend modification or automatic service restart.

Qwen uses core `TextEncodeQwenImage21` instead of Swarm’s questionable empty-latent fallback. Krea/MOSS adapt only the text/vision picture-marker/template behavior into a narrow DEAS node; MIT attribution retained. MOSS uses Swarm’s Krea-style template intentionally, not an asserted equivalent of the native MOSS default.
- **Real GPU reference-effect checks:** six successful 512×512 generations on the local RTX 5090: Qwen Image 2.1 Turbo (`qwen21TurboOfficial_bf16`, `qwen3vl_8b_int8_convrot`, Qwen 2.1 VAE), MOSS (`mossImage_sft_bf16`, `mossImage_sft_txt_int4`, Flux2 VAE), Krea 2 (`krea2Turbo_v10_bf16`, `qwen3vl_4b_fp8_scaled`, Wan 2.1 VAE). All jobs completed; outputs visibly followed the corresponding shape/color, and center-pixel channel checks passed. Plain single-reference inputs used here are unchanged by the later numbered-placeholder fix.

Both installed real tokenizers verify that reversed `input_image_2` / `input_image_1` and a negative-only numbered reference bind the intended images. Internal image drags now carry a separate eligibility MIME marker so protected `getData()` during dragover cannot turn video/audio into a false copy cursor; payload/path validation still occurs at drop.

## Internal image drag/drop correction (0.1.48)

- Two separate causes were reproduced: browser-default image URI data can be claimed by GTK/Wry native file-drop handling, and the native video's full-window `GtkFixed` blocks GTK drag destinations despite click pass-through. `dragItem` now replaces default URI/HTML data with the DEAS MIME payload and copy semantics. The native video layer hides on WebKit's GTK `drag-begin` and restores its prior visibility on `drag-end`, including rejected drops; decoding/playback is not stopped. Native OS-drop handling stays enabled; no configuration/dependency/schema changes.
- Real native debug-app acceptance: `tools/verify_native_media_drag.py BINARY OUT [VIDEO]`, with isolated XDG state, a generated PNG and actual XTest mouse gestures, passed four drags (three accepted references and one rejected header drop), with unchanged prompt text. The optional local 320×180/24 fps H.264 test also passed: native video remained unpaused, its clock advanced, framebuffer hashes changed and GL errors stayed zero across drags. No LD_PRELOAD, hidden-layer diagnostic or injected DOM drop is used by the maintained verifier. Evidence: scratch `deas-drag-native-final-3` (no video) and `deas-drag-native-video-4` (video). These are Linux X11/software-WebKit-renderer checks, not Wayland/Windows or audible A/V-sync acceptance. Earlier desktop probes had focus/timing failures and one disallowed focus-IPC call; the verifier now activates only its PID-matched X11 window and lets the previous drag settle before the next gesture.
- Regression gates: **101 core / 820 UI tests passed**, including a prepopulated URI/HTML payload and thumbnail → prompt → explicit Run fixture validating uploaded reference/strength without editing the prompt. **486 native library tests passed / 33 ignored**, rustfmt and production-library/binary strict Clippy clean. All-target strict Clippy remains blocked by the pre-existing needless borrow at `install.rs:2156`; it was not changed. TypeScript/production frontend build and the normal Linux build passed; the fixed binary is `artifacts/deas-linux-amd64`. Existing Vite chunk/dynamic-import warnings remain.
- Windows WebView2 HTML5/native-drop interoperability and external prompt-file drops (`File.path` fallback) remain known unaccepted paths. No new model inference, performance benchmark, Windows runtime or publication.
