## Image-reference ReVision for SDXL (P2, 0.1.46)

- `imageReference = {visionModel, images:[{image,strength}]}` (1–4 images, per-image strength in `[0,1]`, safe names, no `diffusion` companion) validates and compiles identically in `src/lib/execution.ts` and `src-tauri/src/jobs.rs`. ReVision compiles only for checkpoint (SDXL-family) image recipes; split-loader families reject `imageReference` with an explanatory message pointing to their native paths, which stay untouched (`inputImage`, video References).
- Compiled graph: one shared `CLIPVisionLoader`; per reference `LoadImage` → `CLIPVisionEncode` (center crop) → `unCLIPConditioning` chained from the positive text conditioning through each previous reference; positive inputs that equaled `["2",0]` rewire to the final chain link; noise augmentation stays 0.0. `recipe_prerequisites.rs` adds the four core nodes to the optional allowlist and exposes `visionModels` from the vision loader combo.
- Workbench drops up to 4 media-dock images on the prompt field; each active reference renders a name chip, strength slider and remove action under a shared vision-model picker with the visible "Reference pictures: {count} of {max}" counter. Reference settings and uploads are captured in the immutable Run snapshot; submission requires an eligible checkpoint family, a selected vision model and resolvable upload names. Unsupported families show the localized notice in all six locales.
- Gates passed: 100 core / 819 UI tests, TypeScript typecheck and production build, `cargo test --lib` 485 library + 1 filtered test with 33 ignored (including the multi-reference compile/rejection fixtures), rustfmt clean, strict all-targets Clippy retaining only the separately recorded pre-existing `install.rs:2156` warning.
- Live evidence on a throwaway CPU ComfyUI (`--port 8199 --cpu`, stopped after the check, no user service touched): submitting the compiled two-reference schema returned `node_errors: {}` and completed with `SaveImage` output `deas-revision-verify_00001_.png` (Illustrious checkpoint, shared ViT-bigG tower, strengths 0.5/0.2). The first attempt with a ViT-L (768-dim) tower loaded and validated, then failed inside pooled noise augmentation (`size of tensor a (768) must match tensor b … (1280)`): SDXL unCLIP conditioning accepts only 1280-dim ViT-bigG vision towers. The 4-reference cap is a product limit, not an architectural maximum. No Windows/CI, native-webview runtime or GPU acceptance ran in this slice; no commit/push/tag/release, no version bump (feature slice under the shared Unreleased header).

## Repeated Run and split Run/Stop (Q2c2, 0.1.46)

Completion audit: the canonical plan still described the old polling blocker although both the fix and its regression were present. Fresh independent review found no remaining Q2c2 code blocker, so the plan now records bounded fixture/native-build completion without adding duplicate implementation. Parent reran 217 Workbench/wrapper tests, 17 native Stop tests, the strict fixture selfcheck, 18 Run/Stop browser cases and 15 collection browser cases; independent review separately passed 192 Workbench and 37 wrapper/Continuity tests. Browser cases use simulated IPC, not real cancellation. The new wide/narrow Run/Stop screenshots in scratch `deas-run-stop-dqm41_gd` were inspected; controls remain readable without overlap. The temporary owned Vite process was terminated afterward. No product-source change or new executable build was needed for this closure; the current path-audit build/full-suite evidence above is retained rather than reported as freshly rerun. Native Windows/GPU cancellation and performance gates remain open.

- Repeated Studio Run presses capture immutable image/video/Post-process intents, upload references, output naming and per-press seeds before awaits; Fixed retains its seed, Random advances the next seed synchronously. Run remains Run while queued/running work exists. Adjacent localized Stop stays reachable when the current draft becomes invalid, drops unsent frontend preparations, disables Continuity Run-all and fences stale Run/poll/clip-resolution replies. Actual diagnostic controls and per-job output-root ownership remain separate/unchanged.
- Native current/poll/Stop snapshots add `admissionEpoch` (initial zero), `stopping` and `stopAvailable:true`. Run requires the captured JavaScript-safe generation; admission checks it under the same mutex in which confirmed Stop advances it before invalidating plans. A delayed pre-Stop Run cannot begin preparation/submission after Stop finishes. Overlapping Stops share one advance; unconfirmed, malformed, unsafe or exhausted generations fail closed. The true Stop capability means coordinator support, not permission to interrupt foreign/unsupported jobs. Empty/settled/partial outcomes preserve real job states, products and separate mutation-attempt markers; no global interrupt, queue clear or service stop.
- Shared frontend wrappers retain pending Run ownership by admission generation and coalesce Stop across remounts. A newer confirmed fence makes older outstanding calls ineligible without retrying them. Native preparation/capacity remain native values; unresolved ownership is not fabricated as successful cancellation. Collection polling can still observe existing peers while another local preparation/Run waits; idle backend polling remains disabled and Continuity remains serial/job-ID-bound.
- Integrated frontend gates passed **100 core / 819 UI tests**, typecheck and production frontend build. Parent native job checks passed **117 tests / 2 ignored**, explicitly excluding the concurrent Image-Reference test `image_reference_compiles_core_revision_and_rejects_split_families`. Production-library/binary strict Clippy, Windows-MSVC library crosscheck and diff checks passed. The final complete serial native rerun passed **485 library / 3 binary tests, 33 ignored**. The earlier run had two failures in concurrent Image-Reference fixtures (invalid sigmas and a `visionModels` allowlist expectation); the latest source passes those tests without Queue work modifying that separate feature. Full rustfmt still reports formatting in the Image-Reference test and its prerequisite fixture; that work remains preserved rather than reformatted here. All-target strict Clippy retains the separately recorded pre-existing `install.rs:2156` warning.
- `tools/verify_workbench_run_stop.py` passed its strict fixture selfcheck and **18 Chromium simulated-IPC cases**: empty recovery, three immutable fixed-seed Runs, captured random seeds, keyboard Stop, invalid-draft/pending controls, settled/partial/unreachable outcomes, retained completed outputs, no repeated mock mutations and six locales at 1440/320 px. `tools/verify_workbench_collection.py` also passed **15 collection cases** against the new generation/capability contract. Parent inspected the final `<verification-scratch>/deas-run-stop-0hmlexq9/workbench-run-stop-{1440,320}.png`: adjacent controls are readable without horizontal overflow. The verifier originally tried selecting a nonexistent empty checkpoint option and measuring Stop after it correctly disappeared on settled completion; it now uses a genuine missing-input draft and measures the active split control. Fixtures do not prove native cancellation, physical media operations or model quality.
- Independent native review found no issues. Frontend review reproduced one P2: another Run during an in-flight collection poll invalidated its response without restarting observation when `working` remained true. The fix increments the existing observation-retry state for every Run invalidation and resets it for immediate final reconciliation; its regression publishes peer success while both Runs remain pending, rejects the stale reply and proves no later idle polling. Final independent narrow re-review found no issues: 20 relevant observation/recovery/Stop/remount/Continuity tests passed (131 filtered), plus the regression alone. Final Linux artifact provenance is recorded below. No real model/GPU cancellation, native-webview/Windows runtime or controlled resource/performance acceptance was run; no model downloads, external service changes, commit/push/tag/release or version bump.

## Studio native collection integration (Q2c1, 0.1.46)

- Studio image/video/Post-process and Continuity now call `native_workbench_run` through the typed `runWorkbenchJob` wrapper; diagnostic prepare/submit/status/current remain separate. `readCurrentWorkbench` restores the native-held collection without backend reads or computation, and `pollWorkbench` observes all held jobs against their original endpoints. The UI displays native order newest-first without truncating active/unknown peers. Unknown/preparing/recovery states retain the single-admission guard; repeated Run and visible Stop remain Q2c2, with `stopAvailable:false` unchanged.
- Intents, upload references, masks/geometry and mutable layer canvases are captured before awaits; video upload names replace source placeholders in the captured draft rather than rebuilding from edited controls. Continuity pins/fails only its submitted job ID, not an unrelated recovered peer. Obsolete recovery/poll replies and unmounted callbacks cannot overwrite newer observations; IPC errors do not fabricate peer states.
- The initial independent review reproduced four defects despite the first full/browser passes: wrong current-environment paths for recovered outputs; late uploads submitting after unmount; ambiguous Run IPC failure reopening admission without reconciliation; and immediate-terminal runs retaining evicted native entries. Corrective regressions now cover each. Native snapshots expose optional `outputRoot` only after revalidating the original held directory identity; all Studio local output paths and relocation use it, with rootless job-bound preview/export instead of invented local-path actions. Post-process uploads the actual selected native-root file. Frontend preparation checks liveness before native Run, and every attempted Run triggers authoritative collection recovery before another admission, including lost IPC replies and immediate failures. A final focused re-review then reproduced the narrower sent-IPC-before-native-reservation/remount race. The jobs wrapper now tracks outstanding Run IPC calls across mounts and latches `frontendAdmitting` before/after collection reads; Workbench continues local snapshot observation and guards admission until reconciled. That flag is explicitly frontend-only and never changes native preparation/capacity counts; it does not add idle backend polling. An indefinitely unsettled Run IPC intentionally keeps admission blocked rather than risking duplicate work.
- Corrected integrated gates: **98 core / 799 UI tests passed**, frontend typecheck and production build passed; the complete serial native suite passed **480 library / 3 binary tests, 33 ignored**. Production-library/binary strict Clippy, full Rust formatting, Windows-MSVC library crosscheck and diff checks passed. All-target strict Clippy still fails only at unchanged `install.rs:2156` (`needless_borrows_for_generic_args`), not suppressed. The first full frontend run exposed two App install fixtures that omitted the new session-read schema and strict call expectation; those fixtures now explicitly return/expect the read-only recovery, without relaxing no-generation checks.
- `tools/verify_workbench_collection.py`: **15 Chromium simulated-IPC cases passed**—mixed recovery/all-job progression, immutable metadata/selection/output retention, six locales at 1440/320 px, empty recovery plus one explicit Run, and recovered local outputs using their captured root for image/Star availability requests. Unexpected commands and external backend traffic are rejected. Parent inspected both final screenshots from `<verification-scratch>/deas-collection-j8bqdeed/`; wide/narrow layout is usable without horizontal overflow. The mock initially lacked installer-plan/media-list reads; they were added explicitly. Existing migrated browser fixtures were syntax-checked, not all rerun. These simulated replies do not prove native transport, physical output moves, model generation or interruption.
- Independent corrective re-review confirmed closure of the original four findings; the final narrow review found no actionable issues and independently passed 12 relevant wrapper/remount/preparation/eviction tests (144 filtered). `bash build-linux.sh` rebuilt `<workspace>/DEAS/artifacts/deas-linux-amd64`, SHA-256 `27f5276f1bb2cd6edbc63e22255db383b4bb87e1556135205b73944d3d222964`. Development version remains 0.1.45; this is an unsigned local development build, not a release, and no running application was restarted.
- No real model/GPU/LLM execution, native-webview interaction, Windows runtime or controlled before/after CPU/RAM/VRAM/latency measurement was performed. No external dependency or user service was changed/restarted, and no commit/push/tag/release, version bump or model download was requested. Q2c1 is complete within the stated fixture/native-build scope; Q2c2 is the next implementation slice.

## Native whole-Workbench Stop coordination (0.1.46)

- Q2b3 adds local-main-only `native_workbench_stop({confirmed:true})` in `jobs_stop.rs`, reusing `jobs_cancel.rs` targeted deletion and source-pinned scoped interruption. No frontend endpoint, graph, prompt ID, job list or credential input; no core `/interrupt`, backend-wide queue clear, diagnostic-slot cancellation or service stop. It retains the inspected ComfyUI source/owned-loopback restrictions from Q2b1/Q2b2; no external dependency source was changed.
- Stop closes admission and invalidates unsent preparations/plans under the same held-state locks used by submission. Admitted Workbench preparation/submission now survives Run-waiter loss; uncertain replies remain unknown without retry or guessed/adopted prompt IDs. Stop owns a detached bounded concurrent pass, coalesces overlapping callers and finishes/reopens admission even after all IPC waiters disappear. Existing submission/status/cancellation work is allowed to settle; new per-job polling and separate cancellation cannot starve it. Captured lifecycle epochs remain pinned across pending→running phases and separate mutation-attempt markers prevent repeated ambiguous operations.
- Results extend the existing collection snapshot with `outcome`, `discarded` and per-job `issues`: `empty` means nothing held/discarded; `settled` means all retained jobs terminal, **not** that every job was cancelled; `partial` means unresolved jobs or per-target refusals. A running interrupt acknowledgement alone is still partial. Initially unknown jobs with a known prompt ID reconcile read-only for that invocation; missing IDs perform no backend requests. Completed outputs, cached bytes/completed-reference records and partial output metadata remain retained; this does not add cancelled-product export or restart recovery.
- Fourteen whole-Stop regressions cover confirmation/empty/repeated Stop, targeted mutation counts, foreign correlation, absent/unknown IDs, lost replies, unreachable/unsupported peers, pending→running before/after deletion, lifecycle changes, preparation/submission/poll/Stop races, aborted Run/Stop waiters and terminal cache/reference preservation. Fresh independent review found **no actionable findings**, independently rerunning **14 Stop tests** and **112 native job tests (2 ignored)**. Parent reviewed the integrated source/IPC wiring and ran the full native serial suite: **479 library / 3 binary tests passed, 33 ignored**. The first full run had one failure in unchanged `projects_tests.rs:816`: spawning the current test executable returned ENOENT while other test compilation was active. The isolated rerun and the complete serial rerun passed; that concurrency explanation is plausible, not proven.
- Frontend remains unchanged: **98 core / 772 UI tests**, typecheck and production build passed. Production-library/binary strict Clippy, full Rust formatting, Windows-MSVC library crosscheck and diff checks passed. All-target strict Clippy still fails only on the pre-existing `install.rs:2156` needless borrow, verified in the starting commit; it was not suppressed or changed. Checked 108 local documentation link targets without missing files. Existing Vite mixed-import/chunk-size notices remain.
- `bash build-linux.sh` rebuilt and atomically staged `<workspace>/DEAS/artifacts/deas-linux-amd64`, SHA-256 `1687133f1881784e8180eae1ff156a78f497e8720cedc1605fbe163c2c00926d`. Development version remains 0.1.45; the running user application was not replaced/restarted in place. This is a local unsigned development build, not a published release.
- No real ComfyUI/model interruption, GPU/resource/performance measurement, native-webview interaction or Windows runtime acceptance was run. No user service/job was stopped/restarted, no dependencies or model weights downloaded, no commit/push/tag/release or version bump. `stopAvailable:false` and Studio's single-job guard remain unchanged. **Next: Q2c repeated Run and split Run/Stop integration**, with immutable per-press intents/seeds, six-language presentation and queue-of-three/race regressions.

## Scoped running interruption and status-abort recovery (0.1.46)

- `native_workbench_interrupt_running({jobId,confirmed:true})` is native-only/local-main IPC over a held Workbench job. It shares exact owned-loopback/lifecycle and prompt/client/immutable-graph checks with pending cancellation, but records its own `interruptCancellationAttempted:true` before mutation. No frontend endpoint/prompt/token/graph input, global queue clear, process Stop, automatic retry or core `/interrupt` fallback exists.
- Source inspection and an executed AST fixture of core `server.py::post_interrupt` at ComfyUI `b1a247c9cf9dbfda4b2c0a0c1d50950667eeaa0c` reproduced its target-snapshot/flag-set race: a foreign successor can advance between them. DEAS instead installs its own `running_interrupt.py` via the existing owned-backend custom-node mechanism. `/deas/jobs/interrupt` holds the same core `PromptQueue.mutex` across exact single-running prompt/client/graph comparison and the processing flag set; the inspected successor resets the flag before execution. The per-launch token is native-held, passed only to the owned child, removed from its environment by the adapter and sent in a sensitive header. Wrong/missing authority, browser Origin, malformed/oversized bodies and foreign targets are rejected. Trusted custom nodes are not sandboxed by this mechanism.
- The native launch captures bounded validated source hashes for `server.py`, `execution.py`, `main.py`, `nodes.py` and the installed adapter. Known normalized LF/CRLF hashes restrict support to the inspected revision; exact raw bytes, live owned listener and lifecycle are revalidated for operations. Unknown/missing/changed sources disable interruption without failing ordinary startup. No post-launch scan can grant a capability absent at launch. This is intentionally not broad ComfyUI-version support.
- The Q2b1 independent-review blocker is fixed: shared standalone status polling now uses a RAII reset guard that clears `polling` on waiter abortion and is disarmed under the commit lock. A real blocked-loopback regression covers abort, another status read and subsequent pending cancellation, preserving aggregate/cancellation waiter safety without changing job storage to a second shared service.
- Fourteen native cancellation tests cover both operations, correlation/ownership/support refusals, per-operation attempt markers, lost/invalid/HTML/auth/oversized replies, no repeated mutations, state races, retained output metadata and abandoned waiters. Running acknowledgement alone never becomes cancelled: correlated terminal `execution_interrupted` history is required; still-running remains running and unexplained absence remains unknown. Completed jobs retain their normal product paths. Interrupted partial output metadata remains in the native snapshot, but this slice does **not** add full cancelled-product preview/export UI or restart recovery.
- Parent ran the explicit source-backed CPU smoke with `DEAS_TEST_COMFY_SOURCE=<workspace>/ComfyUI`: **1 passed**. It copies selected source into scratch without modifying the reference, exercises LF/CRLF launch proof, source/adapter changes and the actual pinned `PromptQueue` class/reset statement against a deterministic competing transition thread. No model imports or GPU work. Full native serial suite: **465 library / 3 binary passed, 33 ignored**; two unchanged parallel fixtures failed under combined load (empty descendant-PID read and LLM listing timing), then each passed alone and the complete serial rerun passed. Frontend: **98 core / 772 UI passed**, typecheck and production build passed. Strict production-library/binary Clippy, full Rust formatting, Windows-MSVC library crosscheck and diff checks passed. All-target Clippy remains blocked solely by the unchanged needless borrow in `install.rs:2156`. Independent integrated review found one P2: Python dictionary equality conflated booleans and numbers in the final queue-locked graph check. A RED adapter request with queued `1` / requested `true` reproduced it. The adapter now uses recursive typed JSON comparison, accepts ComfyUI `1`/`1.0` normalization and rejects booleans versus numbers in both directions and distinct adjacent integers above 2^53. Compact and source-backed fixtures pass; the latter now updates the actual held graph under the core mutex because `PromptQueue.get()` deep-copies it. Full native serial tests, production Clippy/format, Windows crosscheck and the Linux build were rerun after the fix. Focused independent re-review found no remaining findings; the actual adapter fixture and 36 additional identity assertions passed, including nested JSON, bool/float and precision boundaries.
- `bash build-linux.sh` rebuilt and atomically staged `<workspace>/DEAS/artifacts/deas-linux-amd64`, SHA-256 `15e630d60c386cb6dea40ec19f8ffd0f20a2fa8b2fb33a7b734c66b95fe81482`. Development version remains 0.1.45; no commit/push/tag/release. No user jobs or running services were interrupted/restarted. Real model interruption, native-webview/Windows runtime and controlled resource/performance acceptance remain unverified. Q2b3 whole-queue Stop coordination is next; Q2c repeated Run/split controls remain deferred and `stopAvailable:false` is unchanged. The adapter is installed only on a later explicit/normal owned-backend launch, not into the currently running user's process.

## Native pending cancellation foundation (0.1.46)

- Q2b1 adds local-main-only `native_workbench_cancel_pending({jobId,confirmed:true})` in `jobs_cancel.rs`. It accepts only a native-held Workbench job, never a frontend endpoint/prompt/graph or diagnostic job. `backend.rs::Lifecycle::cancellation_owner` checks the live owned child/listener, exact HTTP IPv4 loopback endpoint and lifecycle serial before mutation and after readback. A real isolated child-process fixture verifies production ownership acceptance/rejection, stable serial, URL mismatch and post-stop refusal without adding backend HTTP requests. Production cancellation is restricted to the unauthenticated owned loopback core; remote/proxy/bearer-auth cancellation is not implemented and no provider token is forwarded.
- ComfyUI `b1a247c9cf9dbfda4b2c0a0c1d50950667eeaa0c` `server.py::post_queue` is the source contract: exact `{"delete":[held_prompt_id]}`, no `clear`, empty HTTP 200. History/queue parsing reuses the existing prompt/client/graph correlation and output handling. Only accepted deletion plus same-lifecycle absent readback yields terminal `cancelled`; a second history/queue read covers completion between reads. Running/completed transitions remain visible with completed outputs retained. Unknown/running input states reconcile read-only. A held `pendingCancellationAttempted:true` marker survives status recovery and forbids subsequent delete attempts, including after uncertain/lost responses. No interrupt or automatic submission/retry exists.
- Eight parameterized native loopback tests cover positive deletion/readback, malformed/foreign graph/client/ID/history, wrong ownership/lifecycle, missing IDs, submitting/polling races, overlapping calls, abandoned IPC waiters, pending→running/completed transitions, retained output handles, 401/403, lost/rejected/redirect/oversized replies and no repeated POST. Parent review added a RED→GREEN HTTP-200 HTML case: non-empty acknowledgements now remain unknown instead of being accepted as cancellation success. The transport has 3-second connect / 10-second request / 1 MiB response limits and a 40-second whole-operation deadline, with no redirects, retries or environment proxies. Sanitized shared Logs distinguish cancellation and rate-limit refusals/uncertainty.
- The frontend job contract includes terminal `cancelled` and the optional native attempt marker. Six RED→GREEN locale fixtures verify a translated cancellation label and × symbol, no output/star/duration actions, no automatic polling/resubmission, and unchanged Run availability. No cancellation button or wrapper is exposed. A pre-existing Wildcards fixture now explicitly declares its required plugin rather than inheriting another test's mock implementation.
- Final combined checks passed: **98 core / 772 UI tests** (including **111 Workbench tests**), frontend typecheck/production build, full Cargo suite (**457 library / 3 binary tests**, **32 ignored**), strict production-library/binary Clippy, full Rust formatting, Windows-MSVC library crosscheck and diff checks. The known all-target strict-Clippy warning in `install.rs:2156` remains outside this slice; the implementer's earlier intermittent global log-limiter assertion is documented rather than weakened, and final full native runs passed. The initial independent review found a standalone status-waiter cancellation gap; it is fixed and independently reviewed in the [running-interrupt follow-up](#scoped-running-interruption-and-status-abort-recovery-0146).
- `bash build-linux.sh` rebuilt and atomically staged `<workspace>/DEAS/artifacts/deas-linux-amd64`, SHA-256 `d795307037b2e8ee293f04d799cd30f4c390efd44121fe26d7c82f3dabe8b571`. Development version remains 0.1.45; this is not a new release. No live jobs, user files or running services were modified; no commit/push/tag/release. Real ComfyUI/model cancellation, native Linux/Windows UI interaction and controlled resource/performance acceptance were not run. Running interrupt, unsent-snapshot/whole-queue Stop races and repeated Run UI remain Q2b2/Q2c, with `stopAvailable:false`.

## Workbench all-job polling foundation and Wildcards readiness (0.1.46)

- `native_workbench_poll` is a local-main-only, argument-free native command over the existing 20-entry Workbench collection. It reuses the correlated per-job status path, immutable held endpoints/plans and output bookkeeping; it never submits, cancels, polls the exclusive diagnostic slot or accepts frontend job lists. The existing `native_workbench_current` remains network-free. Studio is not wired to these commands yet; repeated Run/Stop remains Q2b/Q2c work.
- Real loopback fixtures exercise concurrent mixed success/transport failure/unknown status, exact intent/plan preservation, no repeat POSTs, missing prompt IDs, terminal/submitting skips, diagnostic exclusion and overlapping polls. Sixteen stalled requests arrive before any reply, finish within one bounded status window, and clear polling flags after the aggregate IPC waiter is cancelled. The admitted native pass owns the gate until it finishes; an overlap returns the current snapshot instead of scheduling another pass. Existing per-job limits remain 15 seconds overall, 3 seconds connect, 10 seconds per request and 1 MiB per response. State/error changes reuse sanitized shared Logs.
- Independent P1 source re-review found the shared image Wildcards readiness omission: an enabled wildcard intent could leave Run enabled without `DaSiWa_WildcardPresetPromptBuilder`, although native preparation already rejected it. Two RED tests reproduced this for MOSS/SDXL; the shared image gate now checks the existing `wildcardNode` constant. Four regressions cover blocking, explicit Disable recovery and preserved enabled intents with the node available. The Run Plan fixture now declares its genuinely used plugin; video still ignores image Wildcards.
- Combined gates: **98 core / 766 UI tests passed**, including all **105 Workbench tests**; typecheck and production frontend build passed. Full `cargo test --locked` passed (**449 library / 3 binary tests**, **32 ignored**); focused implementer runs passed 9 Workbench and 84 job tests (2 ignored). Strict production-library/binary Clippy, full Rust formatting, `git diff --check` and Windows-MSVC library crosscheck passed. All-target strict Clippy still fails solely on the pre-existing needless borrow in `src-tauri/src/install.rs:2156`; it was not suppressed or changed. Initial combined UI run had two H3 fixture timeouts under load and the corrected Run Plan fixture failure; focused and complete reruns are green. The implementer's separate parallel log-assertion run exposed the existing process-global limiter race; its serial rerun and the parent's complete native run passed.
- `bash build-linux.sh` rebuilt and atomically staged `<workspace>/DEAS/artifacts/deas-linux-amd64`, SHA-256 `68c6ac3240725fcba8838aa8e80ce1600f75ad834e5b8589ddacdea5aae3a7d5`. This is an uncommitted development build at version 0.1.45, not a new release. No user application/backend was stopped or restarted; relaunch DEAS manually to use it.
- Independent integrated re-review found no actionable defects; 9 Workbench-native tests, 64 focused native job tests (2 ignored) and 8 targeted Wildcards/Run Plan UI tests passed independently. Aggregate waiter-cancellation coverage does not establish cancellation safety of the pre-existing standalone `native_job_status` path. No real ComfyUI/model/LLM execution, packed-encoder loading, native-webview/Windows runtime or controlled RAM/VRAM/latency acceptance was run. No new dependency, frontend queue integration, targeted cancellation, automatic recovery, version bump, commit, push, tag or release.

## Native Prompt Forge and Chat integration (0.1.44)

- Reproduced sparse streamed tool-index allocation, blocked-request cancellation, nested-writer cancellation and stale frontend-state failures before fixes. Tool slots are bounded at 128; invalid indices fail closed. Loopback fixtures verify cancellation while waiting for HTTP headers, stream chunks and error bodies, plus nested prompt writing, stalled SearXNG web search and retention of partial chat replies. Closing the client does not guarantee a remote LLM server aborts its own computation.
- Native model listing now scans the local folder and fetches the remote endpoint concurrently (`tokio::join!`, remote polled first); list order (local first) and warning order are unchanged. A timing fixture with a slow scan and a delayed peer proves parallel beats sequential, with tolerance for peer overhead. The node-pack listing also runs concurrently with the readiness check and keeps its old semantics: unconfigured still redirects to setup, a configured failure still surfaces an alert. Local GGUF entries are disabled for these tool-based writers with a six-language explanation; H3/Wan/LTX and PeopleDetector retain their node-pack paths. Tests preserve setup redirects, drafts and delayed navigation. Current project controls are captured live; stale chat results cannot target another conversation, and stale reference analyses cannot restore removed images or overwrite newer edits.
- The embedded PromptForge payload is zstd-compressed (`zstd -19`, deterministic) and decoded with `ruzstd` before the SQLite deserialize or JSON parse; a raw override file is detected by the frame magic and read unchanged. Manifest digests pin the compressed payload, so the manifest fixture hashes the embedded bytes. RED first: with compressed files but no decoder, the bundle fixture failed and `--verify` reported a decode error; both are green after the decoder. The updater compresses candidates and decodes before validation, so digests stay reproducible. Release profile gains `lto = true` and `codegen-units = 1`. A fresh scratch environment still loads embedded prompts, Randomizer and both tables without creating a data directory. Tests verify real tag/trait queries, manifest digests and preservation of invalid existing overrides. SQLite integrity checks pass; all 342,748 tag rows, 34,157 character rows and metadata rows match the pinned upstream snapshot after read-only-source backup/journal normalization. [Data provenance](../src-tauri/forge-data/README.md) records digests, licensing and maintainer-confirmed optional-data permission.
- Complete suites: **94 Core**, **709 UI**, **435 Rust library tests passed**, with **32 native tests ignored**. Typecheck, frontend production build, strict production library/binary Clippy and Windows-MSVC library crosscheck pass. The i18n audit retains six-language parity and UI-prose checks while recognizing literal technical examples, shortcuts, preference IDs and nonbreaking spaces. One pre-existing media-star fixture now awaits its actual async IPC call instead of assuming visible markup proves the effect completed.
- Repository Actions were enabled; `Update Forge data` is `active` and dispatched once, while **Verify DEAS** and **Release DEAS** were disabled manually to cut hosted cost (re-enable with `gh workflow enable`). Every hosted job is billing-blocked before start ("recent account payments have failed or your spending limit needs to be increased") since 19 Oct, so no hosted-run evidence exists yet.
- No real LLM inference, GPU generation, native theme/webview acceptance, Windows runtime or controlled end-to-end performance benchmark was run. Existing user services and environments were not restarted or modified. The Linux release executable was rebuilt with `bash build-linux.sh` for the released 0.1.44 (SHA-256 `6ff0c2277fb3ec56c63c34a85b7c01aec1b13d16a5472a47cd8e9132b1e33c0d`, 30,465,320 bytes / 29.1 MiB, `.text` 30,181,941 bytes) after the compressed payload and LTO landed: the same executable was 78,127,344 bytes / 74.5 MiB before, i.e. the embedded data is no longer stored raw. The Windows `deas.exe` was cross-built with `cargo xwin build` (SHA-256 `9a37ba6393ddbea88412dc06b33be7329bd7cd81c05ae85dc12b0442a77d93ad`, 32,955,392 bytes); it is a PE32+ binary needing the WebView2 runtime and is not Windows-runtime-accepted. ELF/shared-library checks report no missing libraries. This does not prove native rendering or inference, and the NSIS installer/portable zip were skipped (no native Windows runner, no `makensis`; `patchelf` absent). Release `v0.1.44` published with the three-asset fallback plus `BUILD-MANIFEST.json`; the tag was pushed to GitHub and the secondary Git remote.

- Per-job preview WebSockets now close with a bounded Close handshake and ignore foreign terminal/progress events after latching their client-directed execution start. Real loopback tests cover Close/pong/binary preview preservation, foreign events, a peer that never acknowledges Close, and availability of the live-view mutex. The complete native library suite, production library/binary strict Clippy, formatting/diff checks and Windows MSVC library crosscheck pass. Unrelated all-target Clippy warning in `install.rs` remains outside this change.
- `bash build-linux.sh` rebuilt `artifacts/deas-linux-amd64`, SHA-256 prefix `d928deeb114c628f`. No user application/backend was restarted, no GPU generation was submitted and no ComfyUI dependency files were edited. The abrupt-close defect is reproduced/fixed; causality or elimination of the reported live warnings is **not** proven. Other clients can still disconnect. Windows runtime/native-webview acceptance remains untested for this change.

## Automatic restart after a confirmed binary update

- The confirmed update now includes automatic close/reopen. `native_app_restart` is authorized only by successful replacement, waits for native generation/downloads/Library scans and uses Tauri's exit/restart lifecycle. Frontend waits for successful backend upgrade and ProjectBar's existing pending-close/save state; no newer binary or failed download triggers no restart. The original executable path is cached before replacement.
- Focused App/backend/project UI tests: **77 passed**; typecheck passed at that checkpoint. Core tests: **94 passed**. Production native Clippy and Windows MSVC library crosscheck pass. A standalone scratch probe atomically replaced its own copied executable, exited PID 44973 and reopened PID 44974 using Tauri's restart primitive. No live user application was restarted.
- Native tests pass in an isolated scratch snapshot with unrelated concurrent Backend dependency-check edits excluded. Whole-working-tree native tests currently encounter that work's private `install::Inner.approved` access; frontend build/tests also encounter its incomplete `DEPS` fixtures and unrelated H3 timeouts. Those changes were preserved rather than repaired in this update. The later full-suite attempts are not claimed green. Full native-webview update/restart click-through remains unverified.

## Companion headers and manual component selectors (0.1.40, shipped in 0.1.42)

- Added optional `LocalModel.component` identities to the existing native scan. The safetensors reader is bounded to 64 MiB headers and validates regular-file inputs, tensor shape/dtype/offset sizes and payload bounds without reading weights. Names/shapes distinguish Qwen3-0.6B, Qwen3-VL-4B/8B, UMT5-XXL, Wan2.1/original-Qwen VAE, Qwen Image2.1 VAE and LTX2.3 audio-VAE/projection. Supported family data maps slot compatibility; recognized incompatible identities cannot pass through a misleading filename. Unknown formats/layouts keep legacy hints or explicit manual selection, not universal compatibility claims.
- The reported renamed Anima filename is exercised in native rename/shape tests and the production React picker fixture; the exact owner-named file was not found under the reference model root. A real read-only `DEAS_LIVE_MODELS=<workspace>/AI-Ressources/models cargo test --lib live_family_scan -- --ignored --nocapture` found 172 files in 912.682257 ms on a warm scan, including real Qwen3-0.6B, Qwen3-VL4B/8B, UMT5, Wan/Qwen VAEs and LTX audio/projection identities. This measures discovery only, not cold-scan, RAM/VRAM or inference performance.
- Shared full-width `ComponentPicker` controls reuse the compact Media selector skin. Text encoder, VAE, audio VAE and text projection each have independent default-off All files flags. The override widens only the relevant scanned category, never auto-selects an unknown file, and retains explicit missing/filtered picks visibly. Missing companions block Run instead of disabling access to a known model's controls. Exact manual names flow into the unchanged image/video intents; native backend/path/capability checks remain authoritative. Existing global preference/reset/project isolation is preserved; baseline project files still omit these optional settings.
- `npm test`: **94 core / 654 UI tests passed**; typecheck and frontend build pass. Native `cargo test --lib --locked`: **343 passed / 20 ignored**; the ignored real-header scan also passed separately. Strict all-target Clippy, formatting checks of the two changed Rust files, and offline `cargo xwin check --lib --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --locked --offline` pass. Repository-wide `cargo fmt --check` also reports concurrent unrelated formatting in `video.rs`/`video_linux.rs`; those files were not reformatted by this change. The final extended existing project-isolation test passes in a focused rerun.
- Parent-rerun `GDK_BACKEND=x11 WEBKIT_DISABLE_DMABUF_RENDERER=1 /usr/bin/python tools/verify_component_pickers.py http://127.0.0.1:4237 <verification-scratch>/deas-component-pickers-final`: **19 actual GTK/WebKit renders** pass (six locales at 1440/768/320px plus a header-only renamed-encoder case). ## Linux Matroska playback fix (0.1.38 follow-up, shipped in 0.1.38)

- Source inspection disproved the external draft's assumed shipped full-runtime fix: DEAS currently embeds only four Ubuntu GStreamer 1.20.3 plugins and never sets `LD_LIBRARY_PATH`. Preserve that narrow host-first fallback instead of bundling/shadowing GTK/WebKit/GStreamer dependencies. The repaired `libgstmatroska.so` contains the complete Ubuntu `1.20.3-0ubuntu1.10` patch series plus `matroska-rescan.patch`; the other three plugins are unchanged. Rebuild provenance, archive hashes, pinned container image and commands are in [the bundle guide](../src-tauri/gstreamer/README.md).
- `tools/test_matroska_rescan.py` compiles the actual extracted scanner with bounded inconsistent seek-IO fixtures. Before the patch it reproduced **SIGABRT at `id == GST_MATROSKA_ID_CLUSTER`**; after the patch all six direction/outcome combinations pass (invalid candidate, failed peek, valid cluster; forward/backward). Rejected candidates rescan with progress and stop at the beginning. This proves the defensive branch, not the underlying cause in the owner's original clip, which was unavailable to this test.
- `linux_startup.rs` defaults `WEBKIT_GST_DMABUF_SINK_DISABLED=1` only with the NVIDIA kernel module and no existing override, preserving empty/explicit values. Plugin-cache errors now use shared Logs with operation/severity and error kind, without private paths. The environment regression failed with missing default before implementation; the logging failure initially reproduced stderr-only delivery. Final startup tests cover overrides and redacted central delivery.
- `rebuild-matroska.sh` successfully built only the Matroska plugin in a disposable pinned Ubuntu container, retaining all distro patches before the local patch and running the scanner test on GStreamer 1.20.3. Host GStreamer **1.28.8** loads the resulting plugin with SHA-256 **`d6d2e64b2523364d16a83d64640f87d64ffc3bd95b94d620236dc05573fdd309`**. No host packages, models or codecs were installed; container-only build packages and checked source archives were downloaded. Apt tool versions are not snapshot-pinned, so no bit-identical-build promise.
- `tools/verify_video_playback.py` uses system **WebKitGTK 2.54.1**, blob URLs and real CPU-encoded 160×96/24 fps/2.5 s H.264 MP4/MKV, HEVC MKV, VP8/VP9 WebM and AV1 MP4/WebM, each silent and AAC/Opus as applicable, plus a corrupt-input case. It checks progressing decoded frames/changing canvas pixels, middle/near-EOF seek, EOF, reopen, bounded errors and WebProcess termination. Original-plugin baseline: **14/15**, HEVC/AAC MKV near-EOF timeout. First patched pass: **14/15**, silent AV1/WebM EOF timeout. Unchanged full repeat: **15/15 passed**, no assertion/WebProcess termination. These earlier timeouts are not erased or established fixed by the repeat. Reports: scratch `deas-webkit-video-AkSeYC/baseline-verified/report.json`, `deas-webkit-video-patched/report.json`, `deas-webkit-video-patched-repeat/report.json`. Probe uses X11/software webview rendering and the NVIDIA video-sink workaround; production Wayland/GPU rendering, audible audio/A/V sync, original-clip DEAS click-through, long clips/resource benchmarks and Windows playback remain open.
- Final native gates: **320 library tests passed, 20 ignored; 3 startup tests passed**, strict all-target Clippy, Rust fmt, debug build and offline Windows MSVC library cross-check passed. `bash build-linux.sh` built/staged version **0.1.38**, `artifacts/deas-linux-amd64`, SHA-256 **`8569b9ab188cd7f3cb491665a87f2279220cc4a499ac7b962a8005226a107898`**. Frontend production build passed with existing mixed-import/large-chunk warnings; the preceding warning-fix entry records the unchanged frontend test results. An isolated native debug instance and the staged release binary each stayed alive and extracted the exact new embedded plugin; no installed backend/config/credentials were restored, and only the owned probe processes were stopped. Replaying the same 15 cases against the release-extracted plugins first had an AV1 silent MP4 worker timeout, then passed **15/15** with the final owned-process-group-cleanup harness (`deas-webkit-video-release-final/report.json`). Keep that earlier timeout alongside the baseline/first-patched EOF failures; the green repeat does not prove intermittent stalls eliminated. This verifies startup/extraction and real WebKit decode paths, not full DEAS playback interaction.
- No generation, model download, external-service restart, source-media rewrite, version bump, commit/push or publication. The earlier warning fix and plan changes are retained in the working tree and included in the local Linux build. Existing running instances remain on their old executable until the user quits/relaunches.

## Project draft window-close guard (0.1.33 follow-up)

- Fix the previously recorded pending-autosave close gap without changing schema or version: the active project bar registers the installed Tauri `onCloseRequested` API, reads current pending state through a ref, blocks normal window close while dirty/saving/hydrating or running a project operation, and allows a later close after saving. Failed saves remain blocked until retry or explicit Close without saving. Delayed listener registration is cleaned up on unmount. No silent save retry or automatic quit is added.
- Listener failure is visible in all six locales. The new bounded `native_project_close_notice({reason})` accepts only `blocked`/`unavailable`, forwards fixed INFO/ERROR diagnostics to shared Logs and rate-limits per cause; raw frontend errors, paths and prompt text are not accepted. Local-main `core:window:allow-destroy` is the exact additional core API used by Tauri’s allowed-close path, not a broad window/default permission set.
- TDD: focused frontend cases first failed to prevent close or show listener failure, then passed. Tests cover dirty state, an in-flight save, saved-state close, failed-save explicit discard, project creation, late listener cleanup and diagnostic failure/redaction. The native diagnostic test first failed because the command did not exist, then verified allowlisted causes, shared source/severity and rate limiting. Full frontend: **91 core / 545 UI tests** passed; typecheck and production build passed. Native: **293 passed / 20 ignored across 3 suites**, strict all-target Clippy, rustfmt check and offline Windows library cross-check passed.
- The updated `tools/verify_projects.py` passed **18 Chromium locale/viewport cases**. It dispatches the real installed Tauri JavaScript close handler through a mocked event/IPC boundary: no destroy before saving, allowed destroy after saving, then normal project close/reopen. Existing global-preference/no-upload/no-submit assertions remain. This is not native chooser or disk persistence evidence.
- Native boundary smoke on the actual staged Linux binary: an isolated XDG profile, unavailable session bus, loopback WebKit inspector and explicit X11/software-rendering probe override exercised a real pending `native_project_open` dialog. A real window-manager close request was blocked by the actual React close listener, the fixed blocked message arrived in actual native Logs, and explicit probe cleanup through the local-main destroy API terminated only the test app. Result: `artifacts/verification/projects/native-close-results.json`. Tauri's real `invoke` property is non-writable; an attempted in-memory project fixture did not replace native IPC, and **no synthetic project-save result was accepted as native evidence**. The unavailable session bus meant chooser completion and a real native project save/reopen were not verified. Captures containing the default home-media listing were not retained.
- Final `bash build-linux.sh` atomically staged `artifacts/deas-linux-amd64`, SHA-256 **`c5788e83e8bac4663e72f823e251355d1a99efff3cf1a049c49f684a8fc9e718`**, matching the native boundary probe. Existing instances require user-controlled quit/relaunch. Existing Vite mixed-import/chunk-size and duplicate fixture-job-key warnings remain. No new dependency, model download, inference, external-service restart, commit or publication.
- Still open: full native chooser/save/reopen and Windows runtime, process-kill/crash/power-loss recovery, confirmation before terminating running computation, durable executed jobs/generated results/provenance and real SDXL quality/resource acceptance. The historical initial-slice entry below records the formerly open normal-window autosave gap; this follow-up supersedes that limitation only.

## Image draft projects (0.1.33, shipped in 0.1.38)

Project tests exercise native filesystem create/save/close/reopen, separate-process writer-lock rejection/release, failed-save and stale/external-manifest preservation, relative asset references, source deletion with retained managed copy, strict schema/bounds, corrupt/oversized/tampered PNGs, symlink rejection and fixed redacted/rate-limited shared Logs. Project import reuses the job-output PNG envelope validator rather than maintaining another framing parser. UI boundary tests cover cancellation, hydration and object-property-order equivalence, serialized autosave, edits during in-flight writes, retry/explicit Close without saving, stale replies/unmount and no automatic job/upload.

The locale-source audit now recognizes valid key prefixes and audits `projects.*`; clipboard tests target their own status instead of assuming it is the only status region. Offline `cargo xwin check --lib --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --locked --offline` passed; this is not Windows runtime acceptance.
- Browser: `uv run --no-project --with playwright python tools/verify_projects.py http://127.0.0.1:4177` passed **18 real Chromium cases**, six locales × 1440/960/320 px. Mocked IPC enforces revision increments and exercises open → edit → autosave → close → reopen, original global prompt preservation, no unexpected commands and no input upload/job submission. Screenshots/results are in ignored `artifacts/verification/projects/`; German desktop and English 320 px captures were inspected, with readable wrapping project actions/status/limitation text and no project-bar horizontal overflow. This proves browser interaction, not OS choosers or native IPC round trips.

Existing running instances retain their old inode until the user quits/relaunches. No live application or backend was restarted.
- Isolated native smoke: fresh XDG directories and an intentionally unavailable session bus prevented restoration of real keyring credentials/remembered environments. A forced X11 probe produced a blank window with GBM allocation failures. An explicit per-probe `WEBKIT_DISABLE_DMABUF_RENDERER=1` fallback rendered the native shell/header and 0.1.33 version; the fresh profile had no approved environment and no project interaction was accepted. No renderer defaults were changed. Captures were tied to the isolated process/window, not the user's active window; only owned probe/Vite processes were stopped. This startup smoke preceded the final PNG-helper-only consolidation and does not certify the final binary's project interaction.

Wait for Saved and close the project before quitting; an edit still awaiting the 500 ms autosave can otherwise be lost. Video playback remains separately deferred. No claim of full project, model, performance or platform acceptance.

## Local video upload W4b2b2b (0.1.38)

- Scope: native local-video selection and upload in Continuity, preserving original files. `native_h3_video_upload({baseUrl})` returns a backend-relative filename or null on cancel. Native video-only magic, regular-file and 512 MiB validation precedes upload. Reads use the same opened file and a size-capped reader; response reads are bounded. This path still buffers the complete file plus multipart body and is not a memory/performance improvement.
- UI: existing `ContinuityVideoSource` adds a six-language native upload action, busy lock and sanitized error text. Cancellation preserves the current draft/receipt; success invalidates the old receipt without auto-preparing. Endpoint/session/running/unmount changes invalidate late replies. Existing shared native upload callers now also report fixed redacted/rate-limited diagnostics to central Logs. Tests cover explicit sequencing, cancel, failure, stale completion, locking, source preservation, HTTP body/name checks, tampering and oversized files/responses.

Existing duplicate job-key fixture warnings and Vite mixed-import/chunk-size warnings remain. `cargo test --locked`, strict all-target Clippy and rustfmt passed: **277 passed, 20 ignored across 3 suites**. Offline `cargo xwin check --lib --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --locked --offline` passed; this is not Windows runtime acceptance.

## Prepared input video W4b2b2a W3c2 (0.1.38)

- Scope: manual exact relative filename of a video already in the connected backend input directory, explicit preparation, separate Use confirmation, saved source/backend identity, first-extension video-kind generation and Next action Forge. No local upload, player, file deletion, new dependency, service restart or GPU execution. Version remains **0.1.32 Unreleased**.
- Source: parent rechecked nodepack HEAD **`1da4fefa3d2683adfc26c46b5d180a1df3ea8cce`**; `h3_continuity/routes.py`, `video_source.py::prepare_video/import_checkpoint`, `pyav_media.py::probe`, `core.py::mode_family/ClipStore.list_clips`, `h3_forge.py::_generate_continuity`. Preparation hashes/probes and stores a content-addressed manifest; backend VAE import is execution-time and has its own opaque cached checkpoint identity. Source changes are backend-hash-checked at execution. No live preparation was performed.
- Native IPC: `native_h3_video_prepare` wired through `lib.rs`, `main.rs`, existing local-main permission in `permissions/api.toml` and `src-tauri/README.md`; no capability expansion. Exact `{filename}` POST, validated backend URL, redirects/retries disabled, 180-second total timeout and existing bounded response reader. Filename rejects host paths/traversal/controls/backslashes/output/temp handles while preserving allowed spelling. Sanitized response requires exact filename, video kind, lowercase 64-hex source/hash IDs, positive bounded dimensions, finite positive fps/seconds and boolean audio. No manufactured frame count. Fixed redacted diagnostics reuse a fifth bounded rate-limit slot.
- Session adapter optionally returns validated `importedVideo` source ID/dimensions/family from backend provenance, omitting it on ordinary clips. Domain source kind and manifest IDs are persisted/validated; old checkpoint plans/graphs remain unchanged. Video kind requires a nonempty manifest ID and maps to `source_kind:video` plus `source_video_id`, not checkpoint `source_id`. Only first-extension intents carry it. Forge accepts both kinds and checks response kind against the request, retaining draft bounds and secret-free HTTP failures. References remain off.
- Pinning snapshots actual submitted canvas/family and uses the completed job’s backend. Exactly one backend-listed import parent must match source ID/canvas/family before its generated child is selected. This does not recreate the cache-ID hash. Missing/ambiguous parents fail closed; an old cached parent outside the bounded 200-entry listing can stop sequencing and may require a fresh session until targeted lookup lands. Serial fixtures do not prove multi-client job-specific provenance.
- TDD: core first failed by omitting video kind, then by comparing a manifest ID directly with an imported child’s parent; malformed saved source kinds were initially accepted. Workbench fixture first lacked the filename control. Added no-source/checkpoint/video Run-all cases prove first-kind forwarding, later checkpoint-only continuation, child pinning and backend-change run protection. Source UI has 41 tests for literal input, explicit confirmation, errors, locking and invalidation. Repeat-use preserves finished links; clearing changes only local plan state. Native tests exercise HTTP mapping, tampering, redirects/limits, source-kind validation/graph preservation, response markers and status/redaction.
- Full Rust first exposed a test assumption that throttled messages remain in the shared bounded log after the concurrent byte-cap eviction test. The new assertion now inspects retained reports without changing production logging/rate limits or error-redaction checks. Final `cargo test --locked && cargo clippy --locked --all-targets -- -D warnings && cargo fmt --all -- --check`: **275 passed, 20 ignored across 3 suites**, Clippy/format clean.
- Frontend `npm run typecheck && npm test && npm run build && git diff --check`: **91 core / 500 UI tests**. Existing Vite mixed-import/chunk-size warnings remain. Build rerun after the scoped Forge-toolbar layout fix.
- Chromium `uv run --no-project --with playwright python tools/verify_continuity.py http://127.0.0.1:1422 artifacts/verification/continuity-input-video`: **36 cases passed**, six languages × 1440/1100/320 px × checkpoint/video. Uses explicit IPC fixtures for preparation/session/Forge only; asserts no selection on Prepare, exact source kind/context, review before Apply, persisted own-step edits/source identity, nested Escape/focus and modal overflow, no generation IPC. Screenshot review caught a toolbar escaping its step because the existing Forge anchor is absolute; a failing owning-step geometry assertion preceded the one scoped static-position override, then all cases passed. German desktop video and English mobile Forge screenshots inspected.
- Not checked: live hashing latency, real backend/LLM/VAE import and A/V continuation quality, source-tail vision, native WebKitGTK/WebView2/Windows, memory/GPU/performance or desktop distributable. Closing ignores stale preparation replies but cannot cancel backend hashing already underway. Local upload, references and targeted old-parent lookup remain future work. No commit, push or release.

## Checkpoint Next action W3c1 (0.1.38)

- Scope: reuse existing `ForgeButton` for the next runnable Continuity action with a known checkpoint predecessor, not for an uncaptured first take or unknown future source. Parent supplies step seconds and exact canvas/source/session/overlap/current action with references empty and `use_references:false`. Apply/undo target only that step; no generation, new IPC, saver, dependency or imported-video support. Version remains **0.1.32 Unreleased**.
- Source: parent rechecked nodepack HEAD **`1da4fefa3d2683adfc26c46b5d180a1df3ea8cce`**, `h3_forge.py::_generate_continuity/generate_continuity_draft`; the relevant Forge/core files are unchanged against the earlier inspected baseline. The backend supplies at most four chronological tail images if model/runtime permits them, otherwise warnings/text context. No real source-tail images or vision were exercised here.
- Native trust boundary: optional H3 continuity is a strict checkpoint-only object with bounded ASCII identities, reserved-session rejection, false references, a 50,000-byte current-action limit and the exact overlap enum. Normal H3 requests omit it and retain behavior. Successful responses must confirm `continuity:true`, `source_kind:checkpoint` and a nonempty bounded `simple_prompt`; an old backend ignoring the request fails closed. HTTP failure bodies are not echoed into errors/shared Logs; saved LLM settings injection remains authoritative.
- UI lifecycle: fit/history includes endpoint/root and full continuity context. Busy transition notifications lock plan edits/run controls, cancellation/unmount owns the exact request ID and late results are ignored. Backend warnings are bounded to eight strings of at most 2048 characters. Nested Escape prevents native-parent cancellation; Escape and Apply restore focus to the hammer.
- TDD: parent integration first lacked the per-step hammer; focused child tests covered forwarding/normal omission, source/backend fit invalidation, review/apply, busy notifications/cancel/unmount and warning bounds. Chromium then exposed missing focus return after Apply; a failing component assertion preceded the shared focus fix. A native regression first accepted an ordinary successful prompt response without continuity markers, then passed with the fail-closed response check.
- Final gates: `npm run typecheck && npm test && npm run build && git diff --check` passed — **89 core / 456 UI tests**; existing Vite mixed-import/chunk-size warnings remain. `cargo test --locked && cargo clippy --locked --all-targets -- -D warnings && cargo fmt --all -- --check` passed — **265 passed, 20 ignored across 3 suites**, no Clippy/format issues. Rust tests include real loopback HTTP fixtures for exact payload/settings, response markers and redacted failures, not a live nodepack/model.
- `uv run --no-project --with playwright python tools/verify_continuity.py http://127.0.0.1:1422 artifacts/verification/continuity-next-action`: **18 cases passed**, en/de/fr/tr/ja/zh × 1440/1100/320 px. Real Chromium exercises explicit source loading, per-step Forge request context, unchanged action before Apply, history reapply, nested Escape keeping Continuity open, focus return, persisted correct-step action and no generation IPC. All model/Forge/session replies are explicit fixtures. German 1100 px and English 320 px Forge screenshots inspected; controls fit without horizontal clipping. Shared inpaint fixture now supplies a generic environment root for the Forge seam.
- Not checked: real local LLM/source-tail quality, live Forge/backend compatibility, native WebKitGTK/WebView2, Windows runtime, idle CPU/RAM/performance or a desktop package rebuild. Imported-video contexts and use-references remain planned. No external service restart, model download, commit, push or release.

## 0.1.31 source and local release gates

Release blocker fixes are limited to Windows CMD control flow, stale artifact/manifest staging and strict minimum-box round trips. Generated edges are repaired only when floating-point translation undershoots the strict 2% side; TS/Rust/node-pack validators remain strict, with no relaxed model boundary. A Linux release smoke test runs mocked build commands but real 7z/checksum operations, verifies tracked/untracked clean guards, old bundle exclusion, old-release preservation, cleanup and actual-target manifests. Targeted and full suites pass. Independent bounded re-review cleared all three blockers with no remaining security or logic findings; the generation/pack validators still enforce the reference node pack's strict minimum. Local builds completed: `./build-linux.sh`, offline MSVC `cargo xwin build --locked --release`, then `tauri bundle --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --bundles nsis --ci --no-sign`. Both executables embed the current frontend `index-DK0Ytnf8.js` and 0.1.31. Portable ZIP integrity passed; the extracted installer executable differs only by the expected three-byte NSS/UNK marker. PE imports require no external WebView2Loader DLL. The Linux executable requires GLIBC 2.39 and compatible system GTK/WebKitGTK/GStreamer libraries; it does not establish the Ubuntu 22.04 baseline. These are unsigned host/cross-build artifacts, not Windows or native-vision runtime acceptance. deb/AppImage and the Windows CMD/PowerShell release path were not run. Older Unreleased slice sections remain historical checkpoints included in 0.1.31, not additional acceptance claims.

## Find people W3b2c2b2 (0.1.31)

- Reference node-pack `1da4fefa3d2683adfc26c46b5d180a1df3ea8cce`, `nodes/h3_forge.py::{find_people,_find_people,_wait_turn,cancel}` and `js/minimax_h3_forge.js` detection identity rules. - Added `PeopleDetector.tsx` using the existing model picker and Forge service. Opening may discover model metadata but never invokes vision. Find is explicit, input-folder-only and UUID-scoped. Results are candidates until Apply; matched counts preserve left-to-right identities. Empty/error results preserve manual boxes; changed context or closing drops candidates and requests cancellation. Scope invalidation runs before controls become interactive. Automatic Cast rereading remains future work.
- Cancel drops results immediately but backend stop is best effort. The node can miss a cancel before request registration; its queue waits before observing the event. Neither an event acknowledgement nor the UI proves GPU work stopped or memory was released. No global interrupt or queue clear is used.
- Native `people` allowlist branch validates relative input path/model/UUID, strips unsupported frontend fields, retains saved native settings and validates ≤32 finite normalized left-to-right boxes with 2% minimum sides. Raw model output/descriptions are not returned. Failure text is sanitized; shared Logs records route and safe request UUID, with INFO for expected cancellation. Existing permissions/command registration do not expand.
- Regression-first: two missing detector UI cases and unknown native route failed before implementation. Further failing assertions exposed Error-object busy-message mapping and 422 busy handling. Tests cover explicit apply, identity retention, cancel/late result, reference invalidation, close-to-cancel ownership, empty/non-vision/busy outcomes; native real HTTP fixtures cover routing, malformed paths/models/UUIDs/responses, box count/order/bounds and secret-free correlated error/cancellation delivery.
- Chromium component IPC fixtures pass in all six locales at 1100 and 320 px: candidate apply is explicit, late cancelled results cannot replace boxes, Cast read/write keeps the detected identities, and narrow candidate controls do not overflow. English screenshots inspected. These are clearly mocked detector outputs; no vision inference was performed. A stale Vite HMR context broke the dynamic fixture after locale edits; restarting only the agent-owned fixture server restored the shared provider and the unchanged test passed.
- Linux native tests passed: 252 library + 3 binary, 20 ignored; strict locked all-target Clippy, fmt and dev build passed. Offline Windows MSVC `cargo xwin check --locked --all-targets` passed after removing the Linux-only guard from the platform-neutral TCP fixture helper (new tests otherwise failed to compile). This is a cross-check, not Windows runtime evidence. Frontend final publication gates are recorded below.
- Not run: real vision/GPU, actual backend stop acknowledgement/termination, native WebKitGTK/WebView2 interaction, resource/performance benchmarks, release packaging or installed-app acceptance. No user service restarted or model downloaded.

- Tests first failed for missing groups/cards (3 expected failures), then passed. The edge case of nine references plus group members separately reproduced Character 11 using a wrong subject group and the missing Character 32 option (2 expected failures), then passed. Regressions cover numbering reservation, invalid groups, ordered Forge payloads, packs, deduplicated cards, Read and omission of removed members from Write.
- Executed the real node-pack `validate_references`, `easy_cast` and `cast_unit` in an isolated Python namespace for all seven groups. Assertions confirm member identities, left/right order (and middle for `group-123`) plus valid per-character Read units. No images were opened, no models loaded and no backend/service contacted. This checks the actual reference implementation, not inference quality.
- Final Linux gates: `npm run typecheck`, `npm test` (**83 core / 366 UI passed**), `npm run build`. Vite still reports mixed static/dynamic Tauri imports and a >500 kB bundle warning. Rust source is unchanged; no fresh Cargo/Clippy/cross-build acceptance is claimed.
- Isolated Chromium component interaction at 1100 px in all six locales: choose `group-21`, next picture becomes Character 3, Read Character 2 and Write preserve group order and `cast`. These use explicitly mocked IPC, not real vision/inference. No browser page errors. English screenshot inspected; this is not full Workbench/responsive or native-window acceptance.
- Not run: real vision/model/GPU, WebKitGTK/WebView2, Windows runtime, UI idle/resource benchmarks, release packaging or service changes. Existing playback/audio and Media-overlay blockers remain. W3b2c2 who/order/people boxes and W3b2c3 pose/frame cards remain open.

## 0.1.30 local release gates

- User authorized including all pending changes and publishing locally built artifacts despite GitHub Actions being blocked. GitHub's failed job annotation reports account payment/spending-limit restrictions; no hosted job started. This is not a green CI release.
- Local Linux gates: `npm test` (82 core / 362 UI passed), `npm run build` (includes TypeScript), `cargo fmt --check`, strict locked all-target Clippy, and `cargo test --locked` (252 passed / 20 ignored). `bash build-linux.sh` produced the local release binary with all shared libraries resolved on the build host.
- Windows: offline `cargo xwin check --locked --all-targets --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` and `cargo xwin build --locked --release --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` succeeded; Tauri cross-bundled the NSIS installer. Linker warnings concern missing external CRT PDB debug information, not failed linking. PE inspection confirms AMD64 and no external WebView2Loader DLL dependency. Portable ZIP integrity and NSIS contents were inspected; extracted installer and portable executable differ only in Tauri's three-byte `NSS`/`UNK` bundle marker.
- These are local, unsigned artifacts, not hosted Ubuntu/Windows builds. The Linux executable directly requires **GLIBC 2.39** plus compatible system GTK/WebKitGTK/GStreamer libraries; this build does **not** preserve the normal Ubuntu 22.04 portability baseline. Windows execution, native dialogs/dragging, GPU model quality/performance and fresh installer acceptance were not rerun. Existing native video-playback/audio and responsive Media-overlay limitations remain.
- Independent pre-release review found four Forge/RefMod regressions; a separate fix pass added overlap/stale-response/cached-library/RefMod-only-pack tests, and a second independent review passed with no security or logic blockers (38 targeted tests passed). Full parent gates were rerun after the fixes; hosted CI remains blocked, not passed.
- Artifact hashes and exact published source revision are recorded in the release's `SHA256SUMS` and build manifest rather than inferred from historical test entries. Regional prompting remains plan-only.

## Video recipes and continuity planner (0.1.27)

- Native-app follow-up: CSP now permits asset fetches in `connect-src` and owned-backend loopback URLs in `media-src`; the previous policy blocked both playback paths. The owner confirmed video starts after this fix, but WebKitWebProcess then aborts with `gst_matroska_demux_search_cluster: assertion failed: (id == GST_MATROSKA_ID_CLUSTER)` in `matroska-demux.c:2216`. **Open crash: playback is not accepted as working; audible audio is not confirmed.** This is a checkpoint, not a playback-complete release.
- Follow-up checks: 78 core tests, 306 UI tests, typecheck and Linux release build passed. CSP regression test preserves narrow asset/loopback access rather than allowing arbitrary HTTP connections. Fetch and media errors are now shown in the caption instead of swallowed.

- Native `video-generation` intent compiled per family (`src-tauri/src/jobs_video.rs`) from the owner's DaSiWa workflow wiring; 7 native unit tests pin the LTX single-/multi-pass, Wan high/low and H3 Director/Guide/Segment/Publish graphs plus the Director's `timeline_data`/`continuity` JSON contract; a fixture test checks native prepare refuses a backend lacking `DaSiWa_EnhancedVideoCombine` and parses `gifs` outputs; `native_h3_session` and `native_input_upload` have bounded fixture tests.
- `tools/validate_video_graphs.py` loaded the real ComfyUI checkout with its custom nodes (no server, CPU, no model weights) and ran `execution.validate_prompt` on all three graphs: `h3: valid=True outputs=['P','7']`, `ltx: valid=True` (2-pass with first-frame guide), `wan: valid=True` (FLF2V with chunking). This proves node names, input names and link types, not generation.
- Frontend: 77 core tests (frame grids 81/121/49/124/56, chunk formula, planner order/clip pinning), 306 UI tests incl. an LTX Image → Video IPC fixture (video intent, 49 frames, `<video>` in the stage) and an H3 fixture (reference upload, I2VA, planner Run all pinning `root1` → `kid1`), typecheck, build; native 236 tests, Clippy all-targets with denied warnings, rustfmt.
- Owner ran a real MiniMax H3 I2VA job (`video/2026-10-18/…_00001_audio.webm`, AV1/Opus, 768×992, 5.18 s) through DEAS; the native job reached `succeeded` with the `gifs` output. A WebKitGTK 2.x probe (`WEBKIT_DEBUG=Media`) shows `<video src="asset://…">` fails with `FormatError` before any scheme request, while the same file over the backend's `/view` HTTP URL reaches `playing`; the stage therefore uses `/view` for job outputs; for dock media (any folder) it fetches the asset bytes once and plays a `blob:` URL, which the same probe plays (4/4 runs reached `playing`; one run hit a transient decoder `MEDIA_ERR_DECODE`, independent of the source). The Options → Output card's label/control grid is a `div` (the `.wb-option > span` flex rule had collapsed it).
- Not verified: real LTX/Wan generation, actual checkpoint capture by the node pack, in-app playback after this change (probe only), Wan LOW-file pairing on real folders beyond the owner's `_HIGH_`/`_LOW_` names. The H3 `continuity` block relies on the node pack's private v3 settings keys read today (`session`, `source_id`, `capture`, `output_mode`, `continuation_prompt`).

## Masked-area inpaint and blur (0.1.22)

- Add opt-in masked-area crop/context (0–512 source pixels, default 8 when enabled) and independent Gaussian mask blur (0–31, default 0/off). Process the context crop at the active model's native pixel budget, preserving its aspect approximately through the exact model grid, then resize/composite back into the original source. Include blur support in the context rectangle. Disabled settings retain the old graph; zero grow still permits a tight crop.
- Both compilers reject malformed crops, out-of-source coordinates, unaligned/oversized processing dimensions and invalid blur values, and native preparation checks every required core node. Existing inserted layers and tiled VAE remain in the crop path. No SwarmUI/custom-node installation or service lifecycle change.
- 27 focused pure compiler/editor tests and 29 Workbench/editor UI tests pass. `tools/verify_inpaint.py http://127.0.0.1:4287` passes with real browser canvases/PNG bytes and fixture IPC, including crop context, zero-grow, blur without crop and six-locale/five-width layout regressions. Screenshots in `assets/deas-inpaint-fixture-{1440,320}.png` are fixture captures. Typecheck, production frontend/release build, Clippy lib and changed Rust formatting pass.
- Independently execute the emitted crop/blur/composite graph through real ComfyUI core nodes in the existing environment's Python with `--cpu`, without a listener or model loading: 256×192 source → 96×80 source crop → exact 128×128 processing tensor and noise mask → 256×192 final tensor. Soft-edge pixels are fractional, the blur agrees with SwarmUI's Gaussian kernel/sigma within 1e-6, and every pixel outside the feathered mask is bit-identical. Source/decoded tensors are explicit test fixtures, not generated results. Headless extra-node initialization reports an unrelated `nodes_replacements.py` PromptServer warning; all used image/mask nodes load and execute.
- Native jobs suite: 25 passing/one ignored. Full native suite: 201 passing/18 ignored/four failures (the three previously documented cache tests and the timing-sensitive invalid-owned-API test, which passes alone). Full core: 67 passing/one existing OptionsPanel literal-placeholder audit failure. Full UI: 281 passing/one existing Installed VAE catalog-selection failure. Full suites are not green; unrelated code was left untouched.
- Read-only live `object_info/ImageBlur` confirms core radius 1–31. UI and both compilers reject 32 and above; zero bypasses the node. Real `execution.validate_prompt` checks accept 1/16/31 and reject 0/32/64 for the ImageBlur node itself, without queueing or saving anything. SwarmUI uses its own node up to 64, plus different default mask thresholding/Differential Diffusion/compositing choices. DEAS provides the requested crop/context and soft-transition behavior, not identical SwarmUI generation semantics.
- No new model/GPU generation-quality acceptance, native Windows interaction or WebKitGTK click-through. Native inpaint retains the existing source-grid restriction; masks/drafts remain unsaved and inserted layers remain opaque. User backends were neither restarted nor stopped.

```sh
node --experimental-strip-types --test tests/image-editor.test.ts tests/execution.test.ts
npm run test:ui -- src/features/studio/StudioImageEditor.test.tsx src/features/studio/Workbench.test.tsx src/App.test.tsx
npm run build
cargo test --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml input_layers_paste_scaled_images_onto_the_source_before_resize_and_inpaint
cargo test --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml input_mask_resamples_only_the_painted_area_and_composites_onto_the_source
# Start an isolated Vite server on an unused port; do not replace a user's server.
npm run dev -- --port 1421
# In another terminal, with the existing Playwright Python environment:
python tools/verify_inpaint.py http://127.0.0.1:1421
```

## Endpoint-only LLM settings and combined discovery (0.1.18)

- LLM endpoint settings sit right of Startup optimizations, top-aligned at desktop widths and stacked below 1200px. Removed the saved model, local GGUF path, context/GPU-layer controls and fallback toggle. Test selection is provider-qualified and per-request only; saving writes `{provider,baseUrl}`. Legacy disk settings migrate by dropping known obsolete model/runtime fields.
- Local language GGUF discovery always scans the configured Models folder's `llm` directory (or the environment default), independent of the external server. Local IDs are relative and validated again before managed-worker execution; nested symlinks, traversal, projectors/mmproj, malformed headers and nonlanguage architectures are excluded. Scan/metadata/response limits apply; a new unlisted GGUF language architecture needs a scanner allowlist entry. Remote discovery uses prefix-preserving GET `api/tags` or `models`; remote errors retain local models and return warnings. No package/model installation or silent substitution occurs.
